As a union the rail *is* the terminal node and the chains end on it. Census, bare boards: **EC32MB 87 / 6 / 0 / 3** (from 87 / 8 / 11 / 3: `Common_VDD` and `Common_LDOin` left the ground cluster — they are `U402`/`U403`'s terminal nodes), **Edge 176 / 29 / 0 / 33** (from 169 / 29 / 4 / 33: the nine sourced chains split), **DS2 22 / 2 / 0 / 0**. Under the reference harnesses: EC32MB from `J203` **4** (92 clusters, 1 escalation — the FET), DS2 **2**, the machine **4** (255 clusters, 65 escalations at build); the Edge board alone under the bench rails 193 clusters, largest **9** (`VIO_16_23`, unchanged), 63 escalations at build (bound 80; the engine half read 72 with the strap). `stub_count` reads 0 on every board (`no_board_contains_a_stub_part`, all three).

   - **The four lints** (`system.rs` `lint_build`, after the fixed point, from the settled states and the declarations — "reaches no source" is the state `Floating`, the fact `FloatingSense` and `PowerNetUnsourced` report from): `RailDown { part, pin, reason }` for every `PowerOut` pin whose net floats unless the pin is another pin's declared reference (an isolated ground is a reference terminal, never a rail), the reason ranked as what the build can see — an input (a `PowerIn` with a declared reference) unsourced, else the output's reference unheld, else any floating `PowerIn` (a ground) as an unheld reference, else `HeldDown` (the part's own gate: threshold, enable, or a soft-start the snapshot is early for; its monitor says which); `UnreferencedDomain { part, pin, reference }` when the pin's net is sourced and its reference's is not; `UndecoupledPowerPin` for a `PowerIn` with no fitted capacitor between its node and its reference's; `MechanicalOnDrivenNet { part, net, drivers }` from `Resolver::driving_pins` (a Thevenin drive on a non-terminal slot on the pad's root). No model-side report path: the build derives every reason, and rule 2's interface is unchanged.

Goldens: the four exactly as the engine half left them (`determinism` 24 passed, no re-bless). Test gate: 50 binaries, 1078 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored.

5. **Capacitors.** Proof: `cluster_handchecks` RC step reads 63.2 % at t0 + τ to 1 µV and the digital sense fires at exactly `round(τ·ln(3.3/1.8))` ns; a second drive before the crossing cancels it (one `Crossing` record); 25 Ω into 10 pF schedules nothing and the bit-grid test passes with that pad cap declared; 100 pF on TX: the byte decodes, edges at +2/+2 ns; DS2 populated filter settles by 5τ_diff with the pair decomposition; `MultiPoleBlock` on an asymmetric two-cap fixture and on a cap-plus-PWL node; a released TX with 100 pF holds `Analog(v0)`; `Harness::leak` reproduces the permanent-start-bit symptom; incremental == full for Settling; `ln_q` matches libm to 1e-9 on 10⁶ points; the macOS determinism leg green; four goldens unchanged.
6. **I2C bench.** `i2c_bus.rs`: 100 kHz round trip, slave stretches and the master waits, two masters arbitrate, rise at 910 ns with 100 pF; a strength change at the same level re-publishes.
7. **Plant-driven edges; caching only if earned.** `MaDSim/src/wiring.rs` BridgeDrive → four `SetEdge` legs (hand-computed differential to 1 µV), the first mutable conductance; an LU cache per (topology, impedance signature) only if the phase-0 bench shows an escalated cluster on a hot path.

## 9. Non-goals, and the answer

Non-goals: transient/timestep SPICE or ngspice; inductor dynamics; multi-pole RC beyond the symmetric pair (one pole per block, with the finding); Newton or exponential device laws (PWL regions only); regulator current limit; pad Schmitt/hysteresis and ADC modes beyond the ROM seed; P2 current-source pull modes; isolator/receiver propagation delay; thermal and parasitics; STEP as edges; membership changes at runtime; a native `bridge_i2c`; deterministic host-PTY runs; migrating MaD's hand-wired GPIO/encoder/pulse seams (unified-drive first).

**Does it make sense?** Yes — and most of it is already the design: R1 makes a pull-up a resistor, R19 makes open-drain a released drive, R8 already specifies the closed-form RC. "A rail is more like SPICE" is right at exactly the granularity that stays cheap: a rail is a declared terminal referenced to its own ground, entering every neighbouring solve as a constant, which is what keeps every solve at m ≤ 8 and the per-edge budget intact. Your I2C example needs three things the engine lacks, none of them a solver: an impedance-aware fight rule (a 15 kΩ pull against a 30 Ω sink is not contention — today it is), P2 pad pull modes, and a pad that reads the net when it is only pulling it — the last is the one the driver actually depends on, and the reviewer found it. **Where it becomes too complex** is exactly four places, and this plan refuses each: capacitors as union-find edges (8→26-net clusters and multi-pole math), Newton or transient device laws (a timestep, convergence nondeterminism), a capacitor on a PWL node (piecewise transient simulation by the back door), and per-edge step trains. With "every component a node" taken literally — no stub tier, no facade — the price is a handful of small models nobody has needed yet (a PSRAM, an inverter, an oscillator, a regulator, a comparator, an opto), each cheaper than the stub rule that excused it, and the reward is one pipeline: a part is a node with behaviour or the board tells you which part and which value it cannot explain. Held to declared terminals, shunt-C closed forms with a stated one-pole rule, PWL regions cold-started in an ordered bounded loop, and switches as build-time unions, it is four primitives (shunt cap, PWL element, terminal, plant edge), one NetState variant, two timer targets, one projection rule, and stubs replaced by models — not a new simulator. The honest cost is the test churn in §3 and §4: those tests encoded the stubs.
## 10. The node ↔ net interface

Reviewed against the tree, against the physics of every interface on the three boards, and against IBIS 7.2, IEEE 1800/1364 strengths, IEEE 1164, SystemC-AMS, SPICE/MNA and Modelica conservative ports.

**Per instant: one message, `Drive`, three encodings, all through the same sequenced command at the producer's own instant (R1–R5).**

| Encoding | Meaning | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| `Thevenin { volts, ohms }` | a linear port; `ohms = ∞` is normalised to *released* at the slot, never ranked | push-pull pads, sinks (open-drain low = `{0 V, R_on}`, high = released), the P2's 1K5/15K/150K pull modes, rails, straps, stuck-at faults |
| `Current { amps }` | a Norton injection with no shunt: stamped as `rhs += amps`, contributes no reachability (a current source into an open loop leaves the node Floating, with a finding) | the eight NSI50010 current regulators, a regulator's input load — the two places the tree already fakes a current source with a resistor; the P2's 1 mA/100 µA/10 µA pad modes were declined (no compliance figure, no caller — §12 item 5) |
| `Periodic { hi, lo: Thevenin, segment: PeriodicSchedule }` | the record's shape (`sil-unified-drive.md`): a declared compression of a Thevenin edge train with the integer `{freq_hz, since, emitted, total}` that keeps the count exact for late joiners. A held segment (rate 0) rests at its `lo` port. Across a coupling capacitor a phase's `volts` are its AC swing whatever its `ohms` — the TCXO's clipped sine is both phases released at DC with `V_pp` in `hi` — and a phase whose `volts` are not a number passes none | the step clock, forwarded verbatim by the isolator; novel by measurement (820 k edges/s), not by any standard |

No `OpenDrain`/`PushPull` kinds. Open-drain is a behaviour of the node — a sink that releases — and every kind added is an interface variant every consumer must understand; IBIS keeps its `Open_drain` model type only "for backward compatibility" and defines it as "do not use `[Pullup]`".

**Per pin, declared once on `PinDecl` and stamped by the solver, never republished** — IBIS's decomposition:

- `idle: Option<TheveninDrive>` — the power-on drive (`None` = released), which retires the release-at-attach ritual.
- `clamps` — always-stamped shunts to the pin's own supply and ground pins (IBIS `[GND Clamp]`/`[POWER Clamp]`). A pad's *driver* strength is its published Thevenin; a pad that needs a nonlinear driver curve is a PWL element part, not a pin table. Precedence stated so the two never disagree.
- `input: { v_bias, r_in }` — a sense pin's own port (IBIS `Input`): the AM26LV32's 12 kΩ to its bias is the mechanism of its open-input failsafe, a regulator's quiescent load is what holds a blocked V_IN near 0 V; without it those nodes come out of an arbitrary 1 GΩ divider.
- `capacitance_pf`, `thresholds` (`V_IL/V_IH` plus a hysteresis pair, declared relative to the pin's supply or reference pin so a brown-out scales them; JESD8C.01's 0.8/2.0 V as the net-level default), and `can_source` — the one static fact a per-instant message cannot carry. It exists for a build lint, `Finding::OpenDrainWithoutPullUp`: a pin that cannot source, on a net where no other source reaches it through less than `WEAK_DRIVE_OHMS`; and every publish is checked against it (with `can_sink`, §12 item 5's record), a drive that contradicts it beyond doubt traced.
- Nonlinear devices are PWL **branches on the component** — `(pin_a, pin_b, curve, control)` — not per-pin curves to a reference: a diode is a branch between two pins, a FET channel is a D–S branch decided by V(G) − V(S), and the per-pin form is the degenerate case.

**Delivered to a sensing pin:** `Sense { volts: Option<Volts> }`, relative to the pin's declared reference — `None` means no source reaches the node, which is a fact consumers branch on (an open enable on the ISO67xx means *enabled*), not an ambiguous voltage. The digital level is the **receiver's** projection through its own thresholds and hysteresis, chosen by its last level — one compare per delivery, which is what delivery already costs; a clock is handed as its two phase voltages, which the receiver projects the same way (one level from both is no edge it can see), and a relative threshold's supply moving re-delivers the pin's `Sense` so the receiver re-projects it. The policy for a voltage inside the dead band is declared per receiver (`HoldLast` or `Unknown`) with no crate default: `level_of → None` keeps refusing to invent a level, as today. Contention and floating stay distinguishable findings. Current into a pin is an instrument (`sense_current`), not the normal path.

**Resolution** is rule 2: sources reaching a root ranked by total ohms; a pull (≥ 1 kΩ) never contends; ≥ 10× loses with a finding; within 10× an m ≤ 2 solve publishes the divided voltage. Ordinal where Verilog's strength resolution is exact, nodal where Verilog would answer X — the 10× and 1 kΩ are embsim constants, not standard values. A root with exactly one reaching source delivers its open-circuit voltage without a solve, even to an analog sense (since §12 item 5's rules task; until then every cluster carrying an analog sense solved whatever its source count).

**Sequencing, so nothing regresses:** rule 2 before the pad pull modes are mapped (today a `{3.3 V, 15 kΩ}` pull against any sink is Contention because the fight rule ignores ohms); rails as real terminals (phase 4) before the sense becomes volts-only (every supply gate reads a NaN-sourced rail's `Pulled(High)` today); ∞ Ω normalised at the slot before it can be ranked; the pulse channel folded in per `sil-unified-drive.md` steps 1–4, since today it is a second interface with its own registration, routing and un-sequenced delivery.

**What this is, in the vocabulary of other tools.** SPICE stamps every element as a Norton pair and solves KCL with a timestep; Proteus VSM keeps a strength-coded logic state per digital pin and bridges to SPICE with interface objects carrying an output resistance; Icarus and VHDL resolve by an ordinal strength table with no voltage; Renode and QEMU carry a `bool` or an `int` per line and bytes per bus, with no strength, no resistor and no conflict detection. embsim's interface is SPICE's linearisation at event instants with Proteus's ordinal shortcut where it is exact — which is why it can see the bugs the transaction-level tools cannot (a fought line, a missing pull-up, a floating input, a stretched clock) at a per-edge cost the timestep tools cannot afford.

## 11. The interface between nodes, as code

A node never sees another node. It sees its own pins: what it declared about them, the voltage the engine hands back, and the drives it publishes. Everything between two nodes is the engine's — resolution, timing, findings — so two nodes written a year apart on different boards agree by construction.

```rust
/// Declared once, at build. Static electrical facts the solver stamps and
/// the engine never asks for again. (IBIS's decomposition.)
pub struct PinDecl {
    pub number: &'static str,
    pub name: Option<&'static str>,
    pub role: PinRole,                   // Signal | PowerIn | PowerOut | Passive
    pub idle: Option<TheveninDrive>,     // the power-on drive; None = released
    pub input: Option<InputPort>,        // { v_bias, r_in ≥ WEAK_DRIVE_OHMS }: the pin's own load
    pub clamps: &'static [Clamp],        // { to: Supply | Reference, vf, r_d }: always on
    pub capacitance_pf: Option<f64>,     // to `reference`; armed by §12 item 6
    pub thresholds: Option<Thresholds>,  // { v_il, v_ih, hysteresis, dead_band }; fractions of `supply` when it names one
    pub reference: Option<&'static str>, // the pin its voltages are measured against
    pub supply: Option<&'static str>,    // the pin its relative thresholds scale with
    pub can_source: bool,                // what a publish is checked against, and the open-drain lint;
    pub can_sink: bool,                  //   neither = an input (a sense)
}
impl PinDecl {
    // digital_in(n, thresholds), digital_out(n), digital_io(n, thresholds),
    // analog(n) — a reader —, analog_source(n), power_in(n), power_out(n),
    // passive(n); with_idle / with_impedance / with_thresholds / with_reference /
    // with_supply / with_input / with_clamps / with_capacitance_pf / sink_only.
}

/// A nonlinear element is a branch between two of the part's pins, with an
/// optional control pin — never a per-pin curve.
pub struct Branch {
    pub a: &'static str,
    pub b: &'static str,
    pub curve: PwlCurve,                 // off = GMIN; on = Vf + r_d, or R_ds(on)
    pub control: Option<(&'static str, RegionTest)>,
}

pub trait Component: Send + Sync {
    fn pins(&self) -> &[PinDecl];
    fn branches(&self) -> &[Branch] { &[] }
    fn attach(&mut self, io: ComponentNetIo) -> Result<(), AttachError>;
    fn start(&mut self) {}
}

/// The one per-instant message. Three encodings, one command, sequenced,
/// published at the producer's own instant (R1–R5). Volts are in the
/// engine's frame; what a pin is handed back is against its reference.
pub enum Drive {
    Thevenin(TheveninDrive),             // { volts, impedance }; impedance = ∞ is normalised to released
    Current { amps: f64 },               // a Norton injection into the net, no shunt
    Periodic { hi: TheveninDrive, lo: TheveninDrive, segment: PeriodicSchedule },
}
pub struct PeriodicSchedule { pub emitted: u64, pub freq_hz: u32, pub total: Option<u64>, pub since_ns: u64 }
impl PinHandle {
    pub fn drive(&self, d: Drive);                                // enqueue; resolved next pass
    pub fn release(&self);                                        // no drive, sequenced like one
    pub fn set_drive(&self, d: Option<TheveninDrive>);            // drive(Thevenin) alias, deleted after the MaD pin bump
    pub fn level(&self, s: &Sense, last: Option<Level>) -> Option<Level>; // through the pin's own thresholds, now
    pub fn thresholds(&self) -> Option<Thresholds>;               // in volts: a relative declaration scaled by its supply now
    // Instruments — a bench's, never a model's normal path:
    pub fn sense_current(&self) -> Option<Amps>;                  // from the last solve; `on_branch` escalates the cluster
    pub fn sense(&self) -> Sense;                                 // a poll, not a delivery: at_ns is the caller's now
    pub fn net_report(&self) -> NetState;                         // the engine's own report
}

/// What a sensing pin is handed: the node voltage relative to the pin's
/// declared reference, or nothing if no source reaches the node; a clock's
/// two phase voltages and its segment beside it (a held one's resting
/// voltage as `volts`); the instant the engine delivered it.
pub struct Sense { pub volts: Option<f64>, pub periodic: Option<PeriodicSense>, pub at_ns: u64 }
pub struct PeriodicSense { pub hi: Option<f64>, pub lo: Option<f64>, pub segment: PeriodicSchedule }
impl PeriodicSense {
    /// The one per-phase rule: each phase entered from the level the other left.
    pub fn levels(&self, thresholds: &Thresholds, last: Option<Level>) -> (Option<Level>, Option<Level>);
    /// The segment, while it runs and its phases settle to two levels — what a consumer counts.
    pub fn rate(&self, thresholds: &Thresholds) -> Option<PeriodicSchedule>;
}
impl Sense {
    /// The receiver's own projection: its thresholds, its hysteresis, its last
    /// level; a running clock by `PeriodicSense::levels` — one level if both phases settle to it.
    pub fn level(&self, thresholds: &Thresholds, last: Option<Level>) -> Option<Level>;
}
pub struct DigitalReceiver { /* a pin and the level it last read */ }
impl DigitalReceiver { pub fn new(pin: PinHandle) -> Self; pub fn read(&self, s: &Sense) -> Option<Level>; }

impl ComponentNetIo {
    pub fn pin(&self, id: &str) -> Result<PinHandle, AttachError>;
    pub fn on_sense(&self, id: &str, f: impl Fn(Sense) + Send + 'static) -> Result<(), AttachError>;
    pub fn on_wake_ns(&self, f: impl Fn(u64) + Send + 'static);   // on_wake: the µs form
    pub fn schedule_at_ns(&self, at_ns: u64);                      // schedule_at: µs
    pub fn schedule_every_ns(&self, period_ns: u64);               // schedule_every: µs
    pub fn with_wake_gate(self, gate: Arc<dyn WakeGate>) -> Self;  // a hosted node's time, through its host
    // Instruments:
    pub fn on_branch(&self, id: &str, f: impl Fn(Option<Amps>) + Send + 'static) -> Result<(), AttachError>;
    pub fn on_net_report(&self, id: &str, f: impl Fn(NetState) + Send + 'static) -> Result<(), AttachError>;
    // Topology, read once at attach (a regulator's divider, a select strap):
    pub fn node(&self, id: &str) -> Result<NetId, AttachError>;
    pub fn resistors_at(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Vec<ResistorAt>, AttachError>;
}
```

**The contract, six lines.**

1. A node publishes only what it drives on its own pin, at the instant it drove it, and reads only the voltage the engine hands it. It never publishes the net's result, never reads by differencing timestamps, and retracts by publishing again (R3–R5).
2. A node never commits state ahead of virtual time; it schedules a wake and acts when the engine arrives (R1). The engine never advances past an undelivered publish (R2).
3. Everything static is declared, everything dynamic is a `Drive`. A pin's strength is its published Thevenin; its clamps, capacitance, thresholds, input load and sourcing capability are declarations; a nonlinear device is a `Branch` on the component.
4. The engine owns resolution: the impedance ranking, the nodal solve, the RC crossing instants, the region loop, contention and floating as findings. No node re-implements any of it, and no node can be given a shortcut around it.
5. A rail is a node too: a `PowerOut` terminal driving a `Thevenin` with the same message as a pad — and only that encoding (a `Current` or a `Periodic` releases it, traced). A drive's volts are in the engine's frame while a `Sense` is against the pin's reference, so a rail published at its sensed ground's voltage holds its ground; a part that drives at a supply it senses across a ground offset, and a port or clamp behind one, is open (§12 item 5, the review).
6. There is no second channel. The pulse train is `Drive::Periodic`, routed and sequenced like every other drive, kept only because 820 000 edges a second was measured; a node that consumes one integrates the segment itself, once its two phases cross the node's own thresholds (`PeriodicSense::rate`; a self-biased input — an inverting gate stage with a plain input fed back from its own output, or the P2's `XI` with its 1 MΩ feedback on — which takes any running segment, is the one declared exception — §12 item 5, the cleanup and the final pass).

**What changes from today's trait**, in one column: `PinKind` (7 variants deciding electrical defaults) becomes `PinRole` plus declarations; `NetState` (5 variants, one global threshold) becomes `Sense { volts: Option }` plus the receiver's projection; `set_drive(Option<TheveninDrive>)` becomes `drive(Drive)` with `Current` and `Periodic`; `StreamRole`/`pulse_tx`/`on_pulse` fold into `Drive::Periodic`; `Component` gains `branches()`. The `Component`/`attach`/`start` shape and the wake scheduling are unchanged — the QEMU P2 node, the flash, the card and the ADC front end port by editing their tables, not their logic.


## 12. Execution order

§8's phases were written before §10 and §11 settled the interface; this is the order the work is done in, and where the interface lands.

0. **Census, baseline, gates** (§8 phase 0).
1. **One pipeline groundwork** (§8 phase 1): registry classes, the deletions, `UnknownPart` with the value, the `stub_count` gate, the value parser, **rule 2** (source-strength projection — required before anything publishes a weak drive), `PinDecl::idle`, the build fixed point. Plus from §11: `Drive::Current` and the `drive(Drive)` entry point, with `set_drive` kept as a thin alias until the last caller moves.
2. **Switches, oscillator, gates, memory, the P2 package, pad strengths** (§8 phase 2).
3. **PWL branches and branch current** (§8 phase 3), with `Component::branches()` from §11 as their declaration.
4. **Terminals, rails, supervisor, isolated domains; `stub.rs` deleted** (§8 phase 4).
5. **The interface** (§11): `Sense { volts: Option }` replacing `NetState` for delivery, the receiver-side projection with hysteresis, `PinRole` plus declarations replacing `PinKind`, `Drive::Periodic` replacing the pulse channel (`sil-unified-drive.md` steps 1–4); with the retirement of phase 1's operating-point precedence (decision (a), the two analog goldens re-blessed), the single-source rule of §10 and DESIGN rule 8 — a root with exactly one reaching source delivers its open-circuit voltage without a solve, even to an analog sense. After phase 4 because every supply gate today reads a rail's projection, and a volts-only sense before real rails would read every rail as down.
   *Done 2026-09-25* — five tasks, one phase: the **periodic drive** (`sil-unified-drive.md` steps 1–4; step 5 with the P2 task), **`PinRole` and the declarations**, **`Sense` and the receiver's projection**, the **resolution rules** (phase 1's decision (a) retired, the single-source rule, input ports and clamps stamped, `OpenDrainWithoutPullUp`), and the **P2 items** phases 2–4 handed on (the 3 ms restart on a wake of the package's own, one entry point for every core's time, the native core's pads through `BankSupplies`, `P2_FAST_OHMS` from the datasheet, the brownout report and a core hold, the current-source modes decided, `PeriodicSchedule` in nanoseconds). §11 is the code: a pin is a `PinRole` and its declarations, the one per-instant message is `Drive::{Thevenin, Current, Periodic}`, the one delivery is `Sense`, and the level is the receiver's. Each task's record follows this one and carries its measurements; this is the phase's.

they publish Thevenin sources, so they are sources: the **static pulls** — `Pull` (`MaDSim/src/main.rs:246`; `p2iss/tests/rom_boot_net.rs:69`, `rom_serial_net.rs:73`, `sd_node.rs:76`), `BenchPulls` (`MaDSim/src/iss_description.rs:139`, each pin at its own level) and `MisoPullUp` (`iss_description.rs:524`, the 15 kΩ `sdmm.cc` asks for) → `PinDecl::analog_source(n).with_idle(Some(TheveninDrive { volts, impedance: 15_000.0 }))`, and the attach-time `set_drive` is deleted (the pull is the declaration, stamped from build); What moves with it: a source is no sense, so these nets no longer ask for a solve — a net such a pull shares with a released pad resolves by projection, and an assertion on the engine's **report** of it may read `Pulled(High, 15 000)` where it read `Analog(3.3)`; what a sense is handed does not move (the single-source rule); the bridge nets still solve, the ADS122U04's `AIN` pins being analog readers of their own. Keeping `analog()` builds and behaves as today, but declares a reader: every publish is traced as contradicting it, and the pulls stay hidden from `OpenDrainWithoutPullUp`. *The senses*: `on_sense` hands `Sense` — `p2iss/src/lib.rs` level pins :1152/:1159 and UART RX :1179/:1185 take a `DigitalReceiver::new(io.pin(..)?)` and `receiver.read(&sense)` (the RX `now` is `sense.at_ns`); `flashnode.rs` (`CS` :121/:122, `MOSI` :132/:133, `CLK` :141/:142) and `sdnode.rs` (`CS` :144/:145, `MOSI` :166/:167, `CLK` :180/:181): one `DigitalReceiver` per pin; `host_pty.rs` :131/:135 and `tests/protocol_on_levels.rs` :176/:177 → `let rx = io.pin("RX")?; io.on_sense("RX", move |sense| … bridge.receive_sense(&rx, &sense))`. MaD's pins declare no reference, so none of its benches needs a ground; a pad whose declared supply moves is re-delivered its sense (the review), which a `DigitalReceiver` re-projects with nothing to do MaD-side. *The step clock*: `p2iss/src/pulse.rs` — the rate is the pin's own drive: `PulseDriver::publish` always publishes `Drive::Periodic { hi: digital_drive(Level::High), lo: digital_drive(Level::Low), segment }` through the pin's `PinHandle` — there is no "no sink" case left (a periodic drive resolves whatever reads its net), so its `bool` return and the whole edge fallback go: `TrainState` (:59), `service` (:241) and `TrainState::advance` (:267ff), the `emitted` counter (:54), the wake-loop block that services it (`lib.rs:1280–1294`) and `P2IssHandle::pulse_edges` (`lib.rs:763`, which would read 0 for ever); `stop` publishes a held segment (`freq_hz` 0), on which a level reader of the STEP net reads the line's resting low; the direction field goes (the wire carries none; a drive reads its DIR pin); `p2iss/src/lib.rs:1137` `io.pulse_tx(..)` → the pin's handle; `p2core/src/smartpin.rs:12`'s doc names a `StreamRole` that no longer exists; and `with_pulse_pins`' doc, `p2iss/src/lib.rs:1024–:1034` ("Each edge is a Thevenin drive the net resolves…", and a link to `P2IssHandle::pulse_edges`, deleted above — a broken link a `-D warnings` doc build refuses), is rewritten for `Drive::Periodic` (one publish per rate change through the pin's handle, the segment the drive folds), and the field comment at :1044–:1047 (the source role routing to a `PulseSink`) goes with the `stream` field it explains (the cleanup). **MaD's native backend**: `embsim_peripherals::pulse_out`'s `run()`, `emitted()`, `set_frequency`, `stop` and the `on_progress` count integrate in nanoseconds where they floored to microseconds, so a count `MaDSim/src/wiring.rs:293–310` reads through `on_start`/`on_progress`/`frequency` at a given instant can move by a pulse — the signatures are unchanged, and the firmware and the wiring still agree with each other; re-check any exact-count assertion in MaD's SIL and e2e suites. *The package*: to sit in `P2Package` — START gate, the 3 ms restart, bank supplies, the brownout hold — `P2Iss` becomes a `P2Core` (today a `Component`, `p2iss/src/lib.rs:1096`): `attach(P2Pads)` taking its pads, pad senses (`P2Pads::on_pad_sense`), crystal and reset subscriptions and `bank_supplies()` (its pads publish through `BankSupplies::pad_drive` with their `WRPIN` word, as `p2-qemu`'s `Bus::pad_drive` does, so they drive at the bank's `VIO_a_b` and at `P2_FAST_OHMS` = 17.99 Ω fast), `start()` anchoring its clock, `reset()` holding it; `P2Core::start` runs **on the engine thread**, inside the package's own wake, never on the starting thread, and a core's wakes — through `P2Pads` or on the net I/O it is handed — are held until then; a guest inside it starts **3 ms after the reset releases** (5.5 ms on the P2-EC32MB from its fingers), so any MaD assertion on a boot instant moves by 3 ms. And the phase-2 record's two open items: `SIL/p2core/src/board.rs:482–485` `sensed()` takes the strong-mask rule — `(strong & out) | (!strong & in_ext)`, `strong` the pads whose **published** drive is under `WEAK_DRIVE_OHMS` (today `(driven & out) | (!driven & in_ext)`) — which the pads need the moment they publish the weak pull modes `pad_drive` maps; and the P59 fiat (`p2iss/src/lib.rs:946–951`, `bus.inner.set_input_level(59, true)`) goes, `p2iss/tests/rom_boot_net.rs` and `rom_serial_net.rs` gaining a `Pull` on P59 in its place. MaD's standalone `McuComponent` (`MaDSim/src/system_description.rs:346`) is unaffected: on a board of its own it publishes from attach at the nominal ports, its wakes direct. *The design note*: `docs/dev/sil-unified-drive.md` — step 5 has landed (`PulseSegment` is `PeriodicSchedule`, anchored `since_ns`, integrated in nanoseconds in 128 bits, read by `emitted_at_ns`/`rebased_at_ns`: its :61 field list, :65–:69 arithmetic, :113 re-export at `component.rs:29`, :146–:149 rename and :318–:322 order-of-work entry are stale); the wildcard audit's citations at **:280–:284 moved**: `nsi50010.rs:254`, `vo2631.rs:268` and `npn_switch.rs:213` are gone (phase 3 made the regulator, opto and transistor PWL elements and `models/src/opto.rs`), `rail_volts` (`isolation/mod.rs:235`) is deleted (the sense task: a supply gate reads volts, `isolation::supply_up`), `contact_volts` is `end_switch.rs:335` and reads a `Sense`; its deletion table (:246–:254) and blast-radius table (:269–:274) cite line numbers of deleted code. Nothing else: `Finding` gained `OpenDrainWithoutPullUp`, lost `StreamMismatch` and renamed `PulseNotCoupled` to `PeriodicNotCoupled`, none of which MaD matches (`MaDSim/src/system_description.rs:239` logs findings); MaD declares no port, clamp or sink-only pin; and `set_drive` stays as `drive`'s alias until the phase after the bump deletes it, MaD's callers moved to `drive`.
   *Review pass 2026-09-25.* Three reviewers read the five tasks together; every high and medium finding was verified against the tree before it was acted on — two were wrong in a detail and say so below — and the low ones taken where cheap. What each found, and what changed:
   - **A swing inside one of the report's bands was handed as a DC level** (medium, `engine.rs` `combine_phases`). Verified: rules 3 and 5 compared the engine's report levels, so a 0 V / 1.2 V clock resolved `Driven(Low)` in both phases and a receiver was handed the high phase's 1.2 V and no clock, while the coupling overlay handed the TCXO's 0.8 V swing as `Periodic { Low, Low }` with both voltages. Fixed as proposed: rule 3 needs one state **at one voltage**, rule 5 one level at one voltage (bitwise, `-0.0` folded); otherwise the root is `Periodic` with the report's levels — one level for both where the swing sits in one band, as the overlay's node already was — and both phase voltages. `Sense::level` projects a running clock phase by phase from the same last level: one level from both is that level (no edge the receiver can see), two or none is none. (The cleanup changed this: each phase is entered from the level the other phase leaves — `PeriodicSense::levels`, the one per-phase rule.) The reference resolver now names the solve's voltage on its element and on-request roots as `resolve_cluster` does — the oracle found the gap in its first run of the fix (a diode cluster whose phases combined on a voltage only one resolver named; the seed is kept in `board/proptest-regressions/engine.txt`). Proof: `engine::tests::periodic::a_clock_inside_one_report_band_is_still_a_clock`, `component::tests::a_receiver_projects_a_clock_phase_by_phase` (4 cases).
   - **Re-review: a losing clock under a reader came out a clock** (medium, `engine.rs` `combine_phases`). Verified with the reviewer's probe: once rules 3 and 5 needed bitwise-equal phase voltages, a periodic source that lost to a stronger static one still moved the solved low phase by millivolts, so a cluster solved on request published `Periodic { High, High }` with the losing clock's segment — a 10 kΩ periodic pull against a 25 Ω hold, phases (3.3, 3.2918) V; the 25 Ω clock against a 2 Ω hold that `a_clock_outvoted_by_a_far_stronger_driver_is_that_drivers_level` asserts `Driven(High)` unread, phases (3.3, 3.0556) V — where the same wiring unread is `Driven(High)`: rule 6's own text ("a periodic pull against a stronger static source loses in both phases (rule 3)") and the resolution-rules record's invariant below (a periodic cluster under a reader combines its phases exactly as it does without one) were both false, and a relay or a counter (`logic_gate.rs` `rate_of`, `stepper_motor.rs` `receive_train`) would have taken an 8–250 mV ripple for a rate. The oracle could not see it: both resolvers combine through `combine_phases`. Fixed as proposed: `PhaseRoot::of` records `periodic_decides` — a periodic slot in the phase's contest — and rule 3 returns the high phase's state wherever no periodic slot decides either phase (under a reader, the high phase's operating point, the voltage the rule already named); the one-voltage test of rules 3 and 5 applies only where a periodic source decided, so the low-swing clock stays a clock. **Decision (the smallest, one the reviewer did not name)**: "in the contest" is `project_root`'s own contest by the same bar — not losing by `ESCALATION_IMPEDANCE_RATIO` **and** no pull against a strong source (its steps 2 and 3) — not "does not lose by the ratio" alone, which would keep a 1 kΩ periodic pull against a 200 Ω driver (5×, inside the ratio, but a pull, so out of an unread projection's contest) a clock under a reader and break the same invariant. Proof: `engine::tests::periodic::a_losing_clock_under_a_reader_is_the_static_drivers_net` (3 cases — the outvoted 25 Ω clock, the 10 kΩ pull, and the 1 kΩ pull against 200 Ω), each asserting the net unread is `Driven(High)`, read is exactly the state and voltage the same wiring reads with the clock's pin held at its high port (no phases, no segment), and the same `Contention` findings as unread; all three fail with the one-voltage rule alone, and the third with the ratio-only test. Gates (the re-review's run on the final tree): `cargo fmt --all` exit 0; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` exit 0, `Finished \`dev\` profile [unoptimized + debuginfo] target(s) in 1m 38s`; `cargo test -p embsim-board -p embsim-boards -p embsim-models -p embsim-cffi` exit 0 — 53 suites, **1 156 passed** (1 153 + the three cases), 0 failed, 5 ignored; `cargo test -p embsim-board --test determinism` `test result: ok. 24 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.13s`, `git status --short board/tests/fixtures/traces` the rules task's two re-blessed traces and nothing else, their diff byte-identical to the review pass's; `EMBSIM_QEMU_P2_BUILD=… cargo test -p embsim-p2-qemu -- --nocapture` `ok. 13 passed`, `ok. 1 passed` ×3, `ok. 0 passed`, `baseline: edges=16901 yields=16953 publishes=16953 escalated_solves=3 start_ns=Some(5500000) wall=0.270s (15.96 us/edge)` — the review pass's counts exactly (the 60 000-state comparison not re-run: the ROM boot's counts did not move); `RUSTDOCFLAGS='-D warnings' cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` exit 0; `npx vibes collect --write` `wrote behaviours.jsonl — 672 behaviour(s), numbers up to BH-775`, `npx vibes lint` `672 claim(s) linted — 0 error(s), 50 warning(s)` — 663 → 672 rows (the new claim's three expectations × three cases: BH-769, BH-772, BH-775, the collector spending a number per case as it does for `sense.clock-projected-per-phase`), every earlier row kept and none renumbered, every row passing; a first collect read 51 warnings (the scene ran 43 words), reworded to 33.
   - **Cleanup 2026-09-25** — the re-review's seven low findings, each closed; the evidence is this pass's report. **(1) A rate is counted only where it crosses the receiver** (`logic_gate.rs` `rate_of`, `stepper_motor.rs`'s `STEP` subscription). A receiver sees a clock only when its input crosses its own switching point every cycle, so every consumer of a rate takes it through one method, `PeriodicSense::rate(thresholds)`: the running segment where the two phases settle to two different levels through the consumer's own declared thresholds, and none for a held segment, for a wave whose phases settle to one level (that level, read on the level path — a 0 V / 0.8 V wave at a 0.8 V `V_IL` is a steady low) or for one with a phase that settles to none (no level). The consumers: the gate's channels; the stepper's `STEP` (a held segment still reaches `receive_train` — it is the stop that folds a running train's final count); and the one the finding did not name, the P2 package's `XI` (`crystal_of`, through the JESD8C.01 pair `XI` already declares — **decision**, the smallest reading of "every rate consumer": the package takes `XI` as an external clock's input, a crystal being passive and publishing no rate, and the module's vendor buffers the TCXO's clipped sine to a full swing through `U101` before `XI`; every bench and board clock on `XI` is rail to rail, so nothing observable moves; the final pass changed this: in a clock mode that turns `XI`'s 1 MΩ feedback on, `XI` is self-biased). The ISO67xx already projected its phases. (The final pass: not so — a phase with no level took the channel's default, so a default-high channel relayed a wave whose high phase sat in the band; its relay now goes through `PeriodicSense::rate`.) **The exception is declared by the build** (**decision**, the smaller of the two forms named): a gate channel whose input node has a resistor to its own output node — `ComponentNetIo::resistors_at` on the input checked against `node` of the output, read once at attach; no new API, nothing on the board's registration — is self-biased and relays every running segment its input carries (the final pass narrowed this to an inverting stage with a plain input, `Config::biases_itself`). The reason is the circuit's, not a datasheet figure: Nexperia's 74LVC2G04 Rev. 13 has no application section and no linear-mode text (its sections run 1–15, general description to legal information; its figures 1–11 are the logic symbols, the delay waveform, the test circuit and package outlines). `R101` (100 kΩ) returns `2Y`'s average to `2A`, so `2A` rests at the stage's own switching point, and `C132` couples the TCXO's 0.8 V p-p onto it as a swing *around* that point; the engine hands a coupled node its source's swing with the DC stripped (§10's periodic row), which a receiver's absolute thresholds cannot place. Not modelled: the stage's gain around its switching point (only that the swing crosses it); and an input with such a resistor that a DC-coupled source also drives hard enough to override it sits off its switching point yet is still relayed — no board has one. Found on the module: `U101`'s second stage self-biased, its first stage and both of `U601`'s plain. **(2) One per-phase rule** (`component.rs`). `PeriodicSense::levels(thresholds, last)` enters each phase from the level the other phase left, starting from the receiver's `last` and running two cycles (the projection has three outcomes and a phase outside the band is fixed, so two cycles reach the steady pair); `Sense::level` is the level both phases settle to, none otherwise, and `rate` needs two. Under `HoldLast` a phase inside the band holds the other phase's level — the finding's example, a high phase inside the band, now reads low from both entry points whatever the receiver read before — and under `Unknown` it holds only within the hysteresis and reads none beyond; where neither phase leaves the band the receiver keeps the level it entered with (`HoldLast`) or reads none. The ISO67xx passes its channel's last input level; its thresholds are `Unknown` with no hysteresis, so its relay does not move. **(3) The supply-move test waits on what it asserts** (`receiver_projection.rs`): the 50 ms wall sleep and the length snapshot are gone; the bank is waited on as published at 3.3 V before the node is driven, so the no-level read is against it, and after the move the wait is for the reading itself — the last delivery at 1.3 V, read high. The case alone 30 times: 30 passed; the binary 30 times: 30 passed. **(4) Figures**: the bullet above carries this pass's crate gate, ledger rows and lint warnings. **(5) `WakeLadder` and `ScriptedDriver`**: in the Open list above, owned by the first phase whose plan sanctions a golden re-bless under rule 7 — none of items 6–8 does today. **(6) The MaD list**: the step-clock entry carries `with_pulse_pins`' doc, `p2iss/src/lib.rs:1024–:1034` (verified read-only in MaD's working tree: the doc's first line is :1024 and the finding's :1025 its blank second), and the field comment at :1044–:1047. **(7) Hygiene**: `board/proptest-regressions/engine.txt`'s two shrink comments name current types (`Spec`'s thirteen fields, `Op::{Rail, Drive}`, `Drive::{Thevenin, Periodic}`, `TheveninDrive { volts, impedance }`, `PeriodicSchedule { emitted, freq_hz, total, since_ns }`, `PwlCurve::Diode { vf, r_d }`), and both seeds reproduce nothing — `engine::tests::incremental_oracle` passes with them read first; nothing changed. Proof: `component::tests::a_clock_phase_is_entered_from_the_other_phases_level` (9 cases: both policies with a phase inside the band, either phase or both, and both phases outside it); `logic_gate_levels::a_clock_on_a_plain_input_is_relayed_only_when_it_crosses_the_thresholds` and `receiver_projection::a_step_input_counts_a_clock_only_when_it_crosses_the_thresholds` (stepped; each fails with the pre-cleanup any-segment check put back — measured: the gate relays the 0 V / 1.2 V wave, the stepper presents it as a train); `oscillator_chain::only_the_oscillator_buffers_fed_back_stage_is_self_biased` (stepped); `logic_gate` units +2 tests, 3 instances (a plain input's non-crossing wave, two cases; the self-biased relay) with the rate-mode case's fixture moved from the TCXO's 0.8 V to a rail-to-rail wave; `p2::tests`' crystal case likewise, with the 0.8 V and 1.2 V waves added as no crystal. `oscillator_chain`'s chain test is unchanged and passes.    - **Final pass 2026-09-25** — the cleanup's two verifiers raised ten low findings; all ten closed, the evidence in this pass's report, and one premise corrected from the datasheet (item 5). **(1) Self-bias needs an inverting stage with a plain input** (`logic_gate.rs` `Config::biases_itself`): the build marks a channel self-biased only where the part inverts, reads no level in its band (`DeadBand::Unknown`) and names no hysteresis, and a resistor joins the channel's output node to its input node. The circuit fact holds only there: on a buffer the resistor is positive feedback, a keeper that holds the input at the rail its output drives; a Schmitt input toggles only where a swing crosses its thresholds, and a Schmitt inverter fed back through a resistor is a relaxation oscillator's shape, not a bias. The 74LVC2G04 qualifies, the SN74LVC1G14 and any buffer do not; `U101`'s second stage is still the one board instance. Proof: `oscillator_chain::only_an_inverting_stage_with_a_plain_input_is_self_biased` (stepped: a 74LVC2G04 inverter, a buffer with its figures and an SN74LVC1G14, each fed back through 100 kΩ, one 0.8 V, 20 MHz swing coupled onto each input through 100 nF — the inverter relays it, the buffer holds its output low, and the Schmitt inverter drives levels and relays none of it — the steady high first asserted of it was the engine's, not the circuit's (the verification, (b)); it fails with the ungated check put back, measured); `logic_gate` units +4. **(2, 8) The ISO67xx relays through `PeriodicSense::rate`** (`iso67xx.rs` `desired_drive`). Both verifiers are right: a phase with no level took the channel's default, so a default-high channel relayed a 0 V / 1.2 V wave on a 3.3 V side as a full-swing clock, and the Cleanup bullet's "already projected its phases" was wrong (noted there). A running wave is now relayed only where `rate` names its segment; any other wave is the level the input reads from it (`Sense::level`, projected on delivery), and the default state where it reads none — the part's answer for an open input (SLLSFJ6G Table 9-2), as for a DC voltage in the band. **Decision** (the smallest that keeps the relay's stop): a held segment — the stop that ends a relayed train and carries its final count, for which `rate` names nothing — is forwarded verbatim where its phases settle to two levels the same way, the rule the stepper already applies to a held segment on `STEP`; one that does not is the level its resting voltage reads. Contract line 6 has one exception. `isolation_bridge` unchanged: 57 events per profile, 6 552 / 655 360 pulses verbatim. Proof: `iso67xx` units +4 (`a_clock_that_does_not_cross_the_input_is_a_level`: the 1.2 V wave the default state on a plain and an `F` part, a 0.9 V wave a steady low on both, a crossing held segment forwarded; the plain case fails with the old rule put back, measured). **(3) A stepper segment is folded once** (`stepper_motor.rs`): `end_train` remembers the running segment it ended (`Plant::ended_train`), and the same segment handed back to `receive_train` is folded from the instant it resumes — the instant it is delivered again, `train_folded` starting at `emitted_at_ns` of that instant `− emitted` (as first landed it started at the plant's last advance, which the proof below hid; corrected by the verification, (a)): the pulses before it were counted before the stop or went out while the input did not switch. **Decision**: a resumed segment carries no delivery lag in stepped mode — its travel is its pulses from its delivery, and `train_end`'s lag shift has nothing to recover; one delivered after the plant already ran past it carries that span as its lag, as a late new segment does ((a)). Proof: `receiver_projection::a_step_clock_that_stops_and_resumes_crossing_counts_each_pulse_once` (stepped, the source driving from its own wakes: one 10 kHz segment at 3.3 V from 1 ms, at 1.2 V from 4 ms, at 3.3 V from 9 ms, stopped at 12 ms — the drive counts 60, 30 + 30; 140 with the fix taken out, measured; since (a) also resumed at 9.5 ms and with no position samples armed) and the unit `a_segment_that_stops_and_resumes_crossing_counts_each_pulse_once` against the segment's own `emitted_at_ns` (four cases since (a)). **(4) Records**: a note where the cleanup or this pass changed what a record says — §2's `LogicGate` and MCU rows, contract line 6, the review bullet's `Sense::level` sentence, the periodic-drive record's wildcard audit and its isolator's forward-verbatim rule, and the Cleanup bullet's `XI`, ISO67xx and self-bias sentences. **(5) `XI` reads through the guest's clock mode** (`p2.rs` `XiMode`, `crystal_of`, `P2Pads::set_clock_mode`; `p2-qemu` `poll_clock_mode`). The verifier's row is right and incomplete, and so the direction built on it: the AC Characteristics' `Cin` row (P2X8C4M64P Datasheet, p. 48) has "Mode 0 : Disabled (1MΩ feedback resistor off)", "Mode 1 : Direct drive", "Mode 2 : Crystal ≥ 16MHz", "Mode 3 : Crystal < 16MHz", and the System Clock `%CC` table (p. 18) gives `%01`, `%10` and `%11` alike "XI status: input", "XO status: 600-ohm drive" and "XI / XO impedance: 1M-ohm", `%00` "ignored", "float", "Hi-Z" — the feedback is on in the direct-drive mode as in the two crystal modes, and off only in `%00`, the mode the chip starts in (RCFAST, `HUBSET #$F0`). So the rule is the datasheet's, not "crystal modes only": `crystal_of(sense, mode)` takes any running segment in `XiMode::FedBack` (`%CC` ≠ `%00`) — `XI` rests at the oscillator stage's switching point, a self-biased input like `U101`'s `2A` — and reads through `P2_UNBANKED_INPUT_THRESHOLDS` in `XiMode::Plain` (`%00`). **Decision**, the smallest form: a core reports the word through `P2Pads::set_clock_mode(word)`; the package decodes `%CC`, re-reads the crystal from `XI`'s last sense, and tells its subscribers before the call returns; the QEMU core reports a changed word in `poll_clock_mode` and takes the crystal again before decoding the word — nothing while the word is unchanged; a core that reports none (the native image, `HeldInReset`) leaves `XI` in `%00`. Not modelled, as for the gate: a source driving `XI` DC-coupled hard enough to hold it off its switching point. Nothing measured moves: the ROM boot runs on RCFAST with `%CC` = `%00`, and `crystal_pll`'s guest selects its PLL with `%CC` = `%00` (`$010007F3`) on a rail-to-rail bench, green. Proof: `p2_package::xi_takes_a_small_swing_as_the_crystal_once_the_clock_word_turns_its_feedback_on` (stepped: a 0.8 V, 20 MHz swing coupled onto `XI` through 100 nF, a core setting `%CC` = `%10` at 4 ms and `HUBSET #$F0` at 5 ms — no crystal at 3.5 ms, 20 MHz at 4.5 ms, none at 5.5 ms, the core told at 4 ms and 5 ms exactly); `p2::tests` +7 (the `%CC` decode, the datasheet's 148.5 MHz example word among them) and the crystal case with the fed-back swings. **(6) The review pass's behaviour-changing lows, each proved**: `receiver_projection::a_sense_handed_at_build_carries_no_instant` (a build with the clock at 5 ms hands instant 0; fails with the inert-link test taken out, measured); `stepper_motor` `a_segment_anchored_after_its_delivery_is_folded_only_to_the_delivery` (the plant at the 3 ms delivery, not the 5 ms anchor, the count from the anchor; fails without the clamp, measured); `rail` `an_enable_pin_no_configuration_reads_is_passive`; `engine::tests::periodic::a_phase_at_an_unknown_voltage_couples_no_voltage_for_it` (a NaN high port couples no voltage for its phase and the low port's 0 V for the other); `supervisor` `an_asserted_output_sinks_to_the_voltage_vss_names` (3 cases; the no-ground case set on the state directly — on a board `VCC` measured against a `VSS` naming nothing is handed nothing, and the verdict is undefined first). The verifier's Open item for `rail.rs`'s enable is closed by (9) instead. **(7) The ledger at the collector's fixed point**: `npx vibes collect --write` from HEAD's ledger (restored once: a write of an earlier wording spent numbers on an expectation id renamed before anything was committed) `wrote behaviours.jsonl — 720 behaviour(s), numbers up to BH-817`, and a second write changes no row — the same 720 rows (the two files sorted compare equal), every number the same, `behaviours.next` byte-identical: the collector's fixed point. Its byte order is not fixed: the collector sorts rows by suite, id and expectation only, so the cases of one claim follow the order the tests finished in, and the second write reordered cases of multi-case claims (at HEAD, 52 of the 130 multi-case claims already list their cases out of name order). 705 → 720 rows: **24 rows renumbered**, as the verifier predicted — the per-phase rule's cases consolidated onto BH-788, BH-797 and BH-806, so the cleanup's four claims hold nine numbers (BH-776–779, 788, 797, 806–808; 780–787, 789–796 and 798–805 are spent); that claim's 27 rows respecified (its `given` and first expectation name the hysteresis) and relabelled `case_1_…` → `case_01_…` (rstest pads the labels of eleven cases), its two new cases six rows on the same three numbers; nine new expectations, BH-809–817 (BH-812 renamed by the verification, (b)); every other row kept, none renumbered or reworded, every row passing; `npx vibes lint` `720 claim(s) linted — 0 error(s), 50 warning(s)` (68 on the first wording — three scenes over 34 words, a claim over 26, and "buffer", which the linter reads as machinery — reworded). **(9) A rail's `EN` reads a clock through `Sense::level`** (`rail.rs` `latch_enable`): a wave whose phases settle to one level through the enable's thresholds is that level; one that toggles the input, or has a phase that reads none, keeps the part's open-pin answer (`floating_enables`), the wildcard audit's answer for a clock with no single level. Proof: `rail` `a_clock_on_the_enable_is_the_level_its_phases_settle_to` (5: the NCP114 on at 1.5 V / 3.3 V and off at 0 V / 0.3 V and off toggling, its open answer; the AP62301 off at 0 V / 0.3 V and on toggling). **(10)** `BOARD_ENGINE.md`'s two consumer lines carry the crossing condition and the self-biased exception; `component::tests::a_clock_phase_is_entered_from_the_other_phases_level` gains the hysteresis instance — 0.3 V under `Unknown`: a 1.5 V high phase over a 0 V low reads low in both (within the hysteresis of the low it is entered from), 1.7 V none — its two phase voltages one case parameter (an eighth argument trips clippy's `too_many_arguments`), the claim's `given` and its first expectation reworded to name the hysteresis. **Open**, found here and recorded (the smallest choice): the 6N137's enable still reads any running clock as open (`opto.rs` `enabled`), the rule (9) retired for `rail.rs` — no board clocks an opto enable; owner, the next pass over `opto.rs`. The QEMU core decodes a clock word's `%SS` without its `%CC`: a PLL or `XI` source selected with `%CC` = `%00`, as `crystal_pll`'s guest does, runs where the datasheet's `%SS` notes need `CC != %00` (p. 18) — owner, the QEMU node's next clock change. Two collector defects, upstream in vibes first and then the pin: `assignNumbers` records no newly assigned key, so a first write gives each case of a new multi-case claim its own number and the next write collapses them; and `serializeLedger`, "bytewise stable" by its own doc, has no tiebreak below the expectation id, so a claim's cases land in test-completion order.    - **Flake record 2026-09-25** — two flakes in `board/tests/isolation_bridge.rs`, both of a free-running binary whose twelve cases shared one paced clock; each reproduced on the unchanged binary and root-caused in an instrumented copy of the tree (scratch only: a wall-stamped trace of every reservation, drive, sense and advance), neither an engine or model defect, and the binary made stepped so neither can recur. **(A) `the_encoder_reaches_the_p2_across_the_isolator` read `Floating` for `Net-(IC16-INA)`** (CI's coverage job on fd4d4fa; the phase-0 and phase-1 flake at the old line 839). A read race, not a settled state: the AM26LV32's `1Y` is a push-pull output whose declared idle is `Driven(High)` — the state the case asserts — and on its way there `U25` releases `1Y` once in every order, because its attach subscribes `VDD` first and the supply's delivery reaches `apply` before either enable has been read (disabled, so released); the `~G` delivery then drives it high. `settled_state` polled until the net read `Driven(High)`, which the idle satisfied before `U25` had run, and re-read it inside that release. Reproduced on the unmodified binary under 8-way load (1 of 200 runs, beside 5 of (B)) and in an instrumented copy with a wall-stamped trace: the poll returned at 357 555 µs, the release applied 32 µs later, the drive high 264 µs after that, and the case failed on `Floating` — the message's 5 s never elapsed — while the engine's own trace shows the net `Driven(High)` from then on and nothing after. Whether a deterministic engine can rest `Floating` from the same inputs at the same instants: no — every run reached one settled state, and an invariant check in the instrumented copy (after every pass, each subscription's last delivery equal to its net's state, and the shared table equal to the engine's) never fired in 400 loaded runs of that copy, runs which reproduced (A) twice and (B) 26 times. The same race gave the one failure of `a_level_transition_costs_one_drive_on_one_channel` seen there (8 against 6: its baseline count was taken when `OUTA` read low, with `IC14`'s other channels' start-up drives still queued). **(B) `a_step_train_crosses_the_barrier_at_a_bounded_engine_cost` counted 54 against 58** (2 of 108 on fd4d4fa; here 5 of 200 plain and 9 of 104 under llvm-cov, 8-way). The window was wall time on a shared clock. Counted by kind, the failing slow profile lacked its last three records — the `P8` indicator `U34`'s `t_pd` wake, its LED drive, the LED net's resolution — and the fast profile gained the wake as its seventeenth record, after its own first drive at the wake's instant (drives before wheel entries at one instant, R11), where the inverter was relaying the new segment again and published nothing: 57 − 3, 57 + 1. The wake was late because the stop's engine, advancing 5 ns to the wake, slept 198 ms in `apply_pace` (measured): virtual time was a segment ahead of the wall, since the case's 200 ms virtual wait had been released by another case's engine at its advance, before that engine's own pacing sleep; the case's 50 ms of wall quiet closed first, and the next profile was stamped at the wake's instant. **Fixes** (`isolation_bridge.rs` only): every case takes a suite lock and re-anchors the clock stepped (`TESTING.md` rules 5 and 9); its system starts with time held, its thread registers as a virtual-clock actor (`Rig::actor`) and releases time; every wall poll is a `settle()` — a 1 ms virtual park, past the longest instant the rig arms (the UCC12040's 750 µs `t_RISE`, SNVSBO5B §6.9; the SN74LVC1G14's 5 ns `t_pd`, §5.6; asserted at compile time), after which the engine holds until the case parks again, so a read is exact; `Rig::finish` asserts no `QuiescenceTimeout`. The step profile publishes each segment at a settled instant and holds it 200 ms of virtual time, the stop included, counting `log.len()` between those instants: **57** per profile at both rates and **18** for the priming change, now asserted exactly (they were ceilings); 6 552 / 655 360 pulses verbatim; `a_level_transition`'s 50 ms wall sleep became a settle; the end-switch probe is read after a settle past its wake; its `LIVE` lock folded into the suite lock. The binary runs in 0.24 s (1.3–1.5 s free-running). **Decisions** (the smallest each): the whole binary is stepped, since the clock is process-global and one stepped case cannot share it with free-running ones; a settle is an actor's virtual park, no engine API; the counts are pinned to their measurements; the settle window is the harness's, derived from the rig's longest armed instant. **Proof**: after, 200 of 200 whole-binary runs under 8-way parallel load, 200 of 200 of each flaky case alone, 104 of 104 with eight CPU hogs beside and 104 of 104 with `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1`; under the llvm-cov build (`cargo llvm-cov show-env`, its own target directory) 30 of 30 sequential, 32 of 32 8-way and 32 of 32 8-way with the hogs. **The two tests the earlier deflake note named**, found by content: `edgeboard.rs` `the_z_ground_jumper_closed_enables_the_encoder_receiver` (old line 676) and `sd_card_spi.rs` `without_a_yield_between_edges_the_card_reads_as_absent` (old line 645), each run in its whole binary 104 times 8-way with only the named case counted — 0 of 104 each; neither changed. The binaries' other cases were not tallied, and both binaries flake elsewhere under load (Open, below). **Open**, found here: `System::start` returns with the attach cascade in flight, so any wall poll in a free-running binary whose expected state is a pin's idle passes early — observed in `edgeboard.rs`, in two cases that poll (`settled_state`, line 512) for an output pin's idle `Driven(High)` and then read `Floating`: `the_unwired_receiver_channel_rides_its_open_input_failsafe` (`U25`'s `4Y` on `Net-(IC16-IND)`, line 798) and `the_rs422_driver_second_channel_drives_the_direction_pair` (the AM26LS31's `SC_DIR+`, line 650). Measured in the verification (c): on this tree 3 of 1 200 whole-binary runs 12-way beside eight CPU hogs, all the first case; on d982742 (a `git archive` with its own target directory) 3 of 1 200 the same way, two the first case and one the second; the final pass's verifier measured the second once in 50 runs 8-way and the first once in 200 8-way with hogs on this tree, the first once in 400 on d982742, and 200 of 200 8-way without hogs on both. Not this pass's: neither the file nor `machine_parts` differs from d982742, which fails the same way (whether it predates phase 5, which edited both, was not measured); the binary runs in the crate gate and CI's coverage job. The fix is `isolation_bridge.rs`'s conversion (a suite lock, `init_mode(Stepped)`, `hold_time()`, `register_actor`, `release_time()`, and reads after a settle in place of every `settled_state` poll), owner the next pass over `edgeboard.rs`. The race's other candidate is `rs422_determinism.rs`, whose recorded signature (twelve runs in 0.2 s, so no poll waited out its 5 s) is this race's — not reproduced here (0 of 160 loaded runs of twelve settles), so `TESTING.md` rule 9's reason and that test's doc are left as they stand for the lead; `fire_due_timers` does not read the time hold, so in a binary whose cases share the clock another engine's advance can fire a held system's armed wake mid-attach (`edgeboard.rs`'s `SettleProbe` at +1 µs) — latent, not observed; and paced mode releases a parked waiter at the advance, before the pacing sleep, so virtual time leads the wall by up to one jump for every engine on the clock. And `sd_card_spi.rs` `a_block_written_over_nets_reads_back_over_nets` flakes under load (line 631, "and came back byte for byte": one byte of the 512 read back differs, a different byte each run) — measured in the verification (c): on this tree 8 of 80 whole-binary runs 8-way beside eight CPU hogs and 0 of 40 8-way without; on d982742 6 of 80 with the hogs, five this case and one `a_fat16_image_built_in_memory_reads_back_over_the_wire` (line 806); the final pass's verifier measured 5 of 40 (this tree) and 4 of 40 (d982742) 8-way under concurrent builds. Not this pass's: neither the file nor the card model (`sd_card.rs`, `sd_card_component.rs`) differs from d982742, which fails the same way (whether it predates phase 5, which edited the file and `sd_card_component.rs`, was not measured). Each of the five cases that builds a bench (of the binary's fourteen) re-anchors the process-global clock paced (`virtual_clock::init(50.0, …)`, in `spi_bench` and in `the_adapter_counts_the_edges_and_bytes_it_actually_saw`) with no suite lock — (B)'s shape, a free-running binary whose cases share one paced clock, the mechanism not traced here; the fix is the same conversion, owner the next pass over `sd_card_spi.rs`.    - **Verification 2026-09-25** — two verifiers read the final pass and the flake record and raised four medium findings; each verified against the tree before it was acted on, the evidence in this pass's report. **(a) A resumed step segment was folded from wherever the plant last stood** (`stepper_motor.rs` `receive_train`). Verified: a resumed segment's instant was `anchor.max(plant.now_ns)` with its anchor the segment's first `since_ns`, so the fold began at the plant's last advance and counted as steps the pulses between that advance and the resume, which the input never switched on; the final pass's proof passed only because its resume landed on one of the drive's own 1 ms position samples and its unit read the plant at 9 ms first. Measured on the unchanged model with the stepped case extended: resumed at 9.5 ms, 60 counted against 55 crossed; with no position samples armed, 110 against 60. **Fix**: a resumed segment's count starts at the instant it is delivered again (`counted_from`), and the plant moves there, never rewound. **Decision** (beyond the verifier's one-line form, `at = max(delivery, plant)` with the fold from `at`, the smallest that keeps issue #43's invariant): where the plant already stands past that delivery — a late, free-running one — the count still starts at the delivery and the span is the segment's lag, recovered by `train_end` as for a late new segment; the one-line form drops those pulses (measured: 6 counted against 7, lag 0 against 1.5 ms). In stepped mode the plant never stands past a delivery, so the final pass's "no delivery lag" holds there (its (3), corrected). Proof: `receiver_projection::a_step_clock_that_stops_and_resumes_crossing_counts_each_pulse_once`, three cases — the resume on a position sample (9 ms, 60), between two (9.5 ms, 55) and with none armed (9 ms, 60); the last two fail on the old fold (60 against 55, 110 against 60, measured); the unit `a_segment_that_stops_and_resumes_crossing_counts_each_pulse_once`, four — read at the 9 ms resume, read at 8 ms and resumed at 9.5 ms, not read since the stop, read at 10.5 ms past a 9 ms resume — the second and third fail on the old fold (8 and 12 against 7, measured), the fourth on the one-line form. **(b) The Schmitt stage's asserted steady state was no circuit's** (`oscillator_chain.rs`). Verified with a probe on the fed-back bench (scratch only; reads after settles from 1 ms to 112 ms of virtual time): `A3` stays `Periodic { hi: Low, lo: Low }` while `Y3` is `Driven(High)` and `U3` has driven once, at 3.3 V; the findings are `FloatingSense` on `A1`, `A2`, `A3` and the two unused `2A` inputs. `R3` joins `A3` to that 3.3 V output, so a real SN74LVC1G14 charges `A3` through `R3` onto `C3` (τ = 100 kΩ × 100 nF = 10 ms) until `V_T+` flips it, and back — the relaxation oscillator the final pass's (1) itself names; the steady high was the engine handing the coupled input its source's swing without the DC `R3` sets. **Fix**, the verifier's: the Schmitt expectation narrowed to what the final pass's (1) decided and the circuit supports — not self-biased, its output driving levels (`Mode::Level`) with no segment relayed (`train_count` 0); "holds its output high" and the 0.84 V reason dropped, and the expectation renamed `schmitt-drives-levels`, since it no longer says the stage reads a steady low (BH-818; BH-812 spent, never committed). The case's three wall polls became reads at one settled instant: time held until the case's thread registers as an actor, then released, and 1 ms of virtual time parked — past both gates' 5 ns `t_pd` (74LVC2G04, SN74LVC1G14; asserted at compile time) — with no `QuiescenceTimeout`. It still fails with the ungated self-bias check put back (measured: first at the buffer's flag; with the flags' asserts taken out, the buffer and the Schmitt stage each `(Rate, 1)` against `(Level, 0)`); 80 of 80 whole-binary runs 8-way. The final pass's (1), `TESTING.md` and the claim now say it. **(c) Two free-running binaries flake under load**, `sd_card_spi.rs` and `edgeboard.rs`: each reproduced here on this tree and on d982742, neither file changed since, so neither is this pass's (whether either predates phase 5 was not measured); recorded with their rates, cases and lines in the flake record's Open list above, where the "0 of 104" sentence now says only the named case was counted and `edgeboard.rs`'s race is observed, not latent. **Decision** (the smallest, the verifiers' own): recorded, not converted — the conversion is `isolation_bridge.rs`'s, the next pass's over each file. **Open**, added: a coupled node is handed its source's swing without the DC its resistor path sets, so an R/C relaxation oscillator — a Schmitt inverter fed back through a resistor onto a capacitor-coupled input, the fixture's `U3` — is not modelled; owner, §12 item 6 (the capacitors phase, which arms RC crossings).
