   *The periodic drive done 2026-09-24* (`sil-unified-drive.md` steps 1–4; step 5, `PulseSegment` to nanoseconds and renamed `PeriodicSchedule`, is left to the P2 task — mechanical, and it churns the peripheral's HAL-facing µs API MaD's `p2iss` also consumes). **There is no second channel.** `NetState::Periodic { hi, lo, segment }` (`net.rs`: `level_of` → `None`; one state per segment, never per edge) and `Drive::Periodic { hi: TheveninDrive, lo: TheveninDrive, segment }` (`component.rs`) are the one encoding; `StreamRole`, `PulseDirection`, `PulseTrain`, `PulseTx`, `ComponentNetIo::{pulse_tx, on_pulse}`, `PinDecl::stream` and `with_stream`, `PinHandle`'s stream field, `Command::{PulseUpdate, RegisterPulseSink}`, `PulseCallback`, `StreamPin`, `CoupledSink`, `PulseRouteSpec`, `Resolver::{route_pulses, add_stream_pin}`, `LivePulseRoute`, `pulse_routes`/`pulse_subs`/`pulse_state`, `pulse_update`/`deliver_coupled`/`deliver_pulse_to`/`route_is_signal_capable`, `EngineEvent::PulseUpdate`, `CallbackKind::Pulse`, `Finding::StreamMismatch`, the isolator's `ChannelRole`/`with_pulse_channel` (and the `PartConfigError::NoSuchChannel` only it raised), the MCU's `bridge_pulse_out_with_direction`/`McuBuildError::DirectionChannelNotBridged`, the stepper's `TrainDirection`/`Config::train_direction` and the gate's `RATE_MODE_DUTY`/`rate_mode_ohms` are deleted; `grep` finds none of the first three names in any `.rs` file. **Resolution** (`engine.rs`): a cluster a periodic slot sits in resolves **twice**, every periodic slot at its high port, then at its low port (`phase_port`; a phase behind a non-finite impedance sources nothing in it, the per-phase form of the slot's released), the existing projection and solve run once per phase and only there — every other cluster resolves once, allocating nothing for the second phase — and `combine_phases` decides each root: (1) two periodic slots that contend (strong, within the ratio) in either phase → `Contention` naming every strong slot, **whatever their segments say** (the note's one ambiguous row, decided the safe way where the resolver makes it: phase is not modelled); (2) a phase that fought (a `Contention`, or disagreeing strong sources that solved — a clock against a comparable static driver) → `Contention`, both phases' fights and the ambiguous level kept; (3) phases agreeing on one state → that state (the clock lost, or reaches only through an outvoted phase; a silenced fight is still reported); (4) a phase with no level → `Floating` (an open-drain clock with no pull-up); (5) one level in both phases → the high phase's state (no edge); (6) else `Periodic` with each phase's level by the engine's own `level_of` and the strongest reaching periodic slot's segment. (The review narrowed rules 3 and 5 to phases at **one voltage**: a swing inside one of the report's bands is `Periodic` with one report level for both phases and both phase voltages, and a held segment names its low phase's voltage — below.) `same_state` compares a periodic state by segment identity, anchor included, so time passing within a segment delivers nothing. The incremental oracle's drive strategy carries clocks (rail to rail, released high; held, 1 kHz, 20 MHz) and its boards coupling capacitors (10 pF, 100 nF): 2 000 random boards, incremental = full, states and findings — it found the one divergence before it could ship (a `PowerOut` slot driven periodic: a rail is not a clock, so `TerminalDrive::from_slot` releases it and it couples nothing; `note_periodic` skips terminal slots). **The AC rule, re-expressed** (`overlay_arrivals`, same rule, same finding, same `oscillator_chain.rs` proof): a periodic slot's AC reach — the roots across one or more coupling capacitors within `COUPLED_REACH_OHMS` of conduction, a declared terminal a barrier, its own cluster excluded — is walked once per topology version (`Resolver::ensure_reach`); every root it reaches publishes `Periodic` with the source's segment and the levels its two ports project to (`level_of_volts`: the swing a capacitor passes, not the DC bias it blocks) **over** its own cluster's state, judged per crossing at the segment's rate (`1/(2π·f·C) ≤ R_far / COUPLING_REACTANCE_RATIO`, else `PeriodicNotCoupled`, now raised by the pass that resolves the far node — at build too — and deduped by the bus); a held segment crosses unconditionally; a far root its own sources fought takes no overlay; two rates on one root, or a rate arriving where a periodic slot of the far cluster contends by conduction, are `Contention` naming every slot — a periodic *pull* there (a self-biased stage's feedback resistor) yields. The overlay is part of the far cluster's resolution: a periodic slot's drive change dirties the clusters its reach touches (`mark_coupled_dirty`, the coupling rule's fan-out, as a terminal's change dirties its dependents), and a dirty pass none of whose clusters any clock reaches pays one scan of its dirty list (`reach_clusters`) — measured: the ROM boot's per-edge cost rose 15 % with the reach read per pass and is back within 4 % of phase 4 with the reject (debug, this host, 0.258 s → 0.267 s over 16 901 edges). A cluster a periodic slot sits in reports no current anywhere (a square wave has two operating points). The event log writes a periodic drive `periodic hi=<uv>@<mohm> lo=… freq=…hz emitted=… total=… since=…us` and a periodic state `periodic:<hi>:<lo>:freq=…`; the `reroute epoch=` record stays (it is every golden's second line; `publish_topology_epoch`). **The wildcards, audited explicitly** (every `NetState` match now names `Periodic`): a supply pin reading a clock is down (`isolation::supply_up`, `ads122u04_component::supply_ok`, `rail.rs` `read_input`/`read_reference`, `supervisor.rs` `read_volts`, `p2.rs` `named_volts`), a clock on an enable keeps the part's own no-level answer (`rail.rs`'s `floating_enables`, the 6N137's open-enable row; the final pass changed this for `rail.rs`: a clock on `EN` is the level its phases settle to, `Sense::level`, and the part's own answer only where they settle to none or to two — the 6N137's is unchanged, Open), a logic input gets no level (`threshold_level`, `digital_level`, the gate's `project`, `level_of` for `psram.rs`, the QEMU pads and the serial deframer), `rail_volts` returns its nominal only behind `supply_up`'s refusal, `~RESET` fed a clock is not high. **The producers and consumers**: the pulse bridge drives the STEP pin `Periodic` between `digital_drive(High)`/`digital_drive(Low)` (the ports every bridged GPIO of the native core drives) around the peripheral's own segment, the bank's segment at attach and a held one at stop; **the wire carries no direction** — a step/direction drive reads its DIR pin, which the firmware drives through a bridged GPIO, so a reversal re-publishes nothing and costs no pulse of phase (`pulse_bridge.rs`'s reversal now folds the one segment at the instants DIR changed and sums to `pulse_out::emitted` exactly, where the re-anchoring relay was allowed two pulses). The stepper integrates the segment its STEP sense is handed, ignores the same segment re-delivered under new levels, and ends the train at the instant its STEP net stops carrying one (released, or fought). **The isolator's forward-verbatim rule, decided**: a channel whose input net carries a square wave drives its output `Periodic` between its own output side's rail and ground behind `output_impedance_ohms`, around the input's segment **verbatim** — the source's count and anchor, never re-anchored at the instant the isolator re-drove, so a supply that moves re-drives the ports and leaves the count alone; a wave whose phases project to one level crosses as that level, a channel that stops passing presents its default state and the clock stops at the barrier (`relayed_segment`; `drive_count` counts level drives, `train_count` periodic ones). (The final pass narrowed this: a running wave is relayed only where its phases cross the channel's thresholds, `PeriodicSense::rate`, and a held one where its phases would; any other wave is the level the input reads from it, or the default state where it reads none.) The oscillator's two phases are **released** (the TG2520SMN names no DC level or source impedance — rule 6; the TCXO's net still floats at DC) and swing `TG2520SMN_VPP_MIN_VOLTS` = 0.8 V (`V_pp` min, "GND level (DC cut)") above 0 V; this departs from the task's "a Periodic drive at VCC·duty through its output impedance", which needs a DC port and a duty the datasheet does not give. The gate's rate mode relays the input's segment between its datasheet ports (`V_CC` behind `R_OH`, 0 V behind `R_OL`) at once, inversion and duty not modelled; the time-average drive is gone. The P2 package's `XI` is a sense: `crystal_of(NetState)` is a periodic net's rate, `crystal_hz()` and the core's subscription are told on a change of crystal only. **What moved, and why**: `oscillator_chain` asserts the chain's square wave where it asserted a mid-rail rest — `Net-(U101-2A)` `Periodic { Low, Low }` (the TCXO's 0.8 V swing), `Net-(U101-2Y)` and `XTAL_XI` `Periodic { High, Low }`, all with the TCXO's segment, `Net-(X100-OUT)` `Floating` — because the net carries the rate now and a time-average was the stand-in for one it could not; one drive per stage, 3 solves, 20 MHz on the package all hold; the coupling fixtures' DC claim is asserted on the refused/shunted cases, where it is observable, and two clocks meeting across an undeclared node are `Contention` on the first clock's net naming both (was `StreamMismatch`). `pulse_bridge.rs`: 31 → **46** engine events for the four-segment profile (5 periodic drives), still under its 64 ceiling. `isolation_bridge.rs`: the line is primed as a held clock before the measurement (the level-to-clock change is the level path's, paid once), then **65 events per profile at 8 192 Hz and at 819 200 Hz alike** (16 per running rate change — the drive, both sides' nets resolved and sensed, the relay, the `P8` indicator inverter's relay onto its LED, `U24`'s four unchanged drives; 17 for the stop), `RELAY_EVENT_CEILING` 32 → 65 with that breakdown in the test (**57** since the review: the harness `U24` re-issues no unchanged drive); 6 552 / 655 360 pulses verbatim, 12 drives for the settled isolator. New stepped case `pulse_bridge_stepped::a_step_line_fought_by_a_stuck_driver_is_contention`: the clock reaches the drive, then a stuck-low pin at 2 ms makes the line `Contention` naming `MCU.P8` and `STUCK.OUT`, and the drive is handed no rate after. Engine unit cases `engine::tests::periodic` (7). Census 87/6/0/3, 176/29/0/33, 22/2/0/0 unchanged; the digital goldens byte-identical; ROM boot 16 901 edges, 16 953 yields/publishes, 5 escalated solves, START at 2.5 ms, crystal 20 MHz. **Open, with an owner**: the escalation count is 5 in 39 of 40 runs and 6 in one — measured at phase 4 too (1 of 60): the P2 pads' `DeclareRead`s arrive in one or two drain batches depending on the attaching thread, and each batch's full pass re-solves the FET cluster at t = 0 — a pre-existing attach race, not a periodic cost (a fix belongs to the interface task: read declarations on `PinDecl`, not at subscription); the test harness's AM26LS31 (`board/tests/machine_parts`) releases its outputs for an input with no level, so a clock stops at `U24` rather than reaching `SC_PUL±` — it needs the isolator's relay rule when a machine test runs a step train through it; `p2iss` (MaD) still publishes a pulse channel and must move to `Drive::Periodic` at the pin bump.
   *`PinRole` and the declarations done 2026-09-24* (declarations only: delivery stays `NetState` until the sense task, and nothing observable moves). **There are no pin kinds.** `PinKind`, `IdleDrive` and its `KindDefault`, `PinDecl::{drive_impedance, new}`, `PinReference` and `Component::references()` (phase 4's decision (5)) are deleted, and `PartClass::Registered` loses `references`; `grep` finds none of `PinKind`, `IdleDrive`, `PinReference` in any `.rs` file. `PinDecl` is §11's — `role: PinRole::{Signal, PowerIn, PowerOut, Passive}`, `idle: Option<TheveninDrive>`, `input: Option<InputPort { v_bias, r_in }>`, `clamps: &'static [Clamp { to: ClampRail::{Supply, Reference}, vf, r_d }]`, `capacitance_pf`, `thresholds: Option<Thresholds { v_il, v_ih, hysteresis }>`, `reference`, `supply`, `can_source` — and `can_sink`, decision (1). The six constructors (eight since the review: `digital_io`, `analog_source`) are the migration path, so every table in the tree ported by editing constructors: `digital_in(n, thresholds)` takes its thresholds; `digital_out(n)` idles high at `LOGIC_HIGH_VOLTS` behind 25 Ω (the old kind default, now the constructor's, `with_impedance`/`with_idle` to override), `sink_only()` makes it an open drain; `analog(n)`; `power_in(n)`; `power_out(n)` idles at the unmodelled rail (NaN behind 0 Ω, what the kind default was) and `with_idle(None)` releases it, as every regulator declares; `passive(n)`; the `with_*` builders carry the rest. `JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS` (JESD8C.01's 0.8/2.0 V, absolute) is a named constant a caller passes with its reason, never a constructor's default. The build validates the declarations against the pin table (`component.rs` `validate_declarations`, `board.rs` `validate_pin_declarations`, both routes): an idle drive on a power-in or passive pin is `IdleOnSlotlessPin` as before; a reference or supply naming a pin the part does not declare, or the pin itself, is `PinFacadeMismatch` naming the identity, as a branch's is; thresholds a pin cannot hold and a clamp to a rail it does not declare are `BoardError::InvalidDeclaration { reference, pin, reason }` (new). **Decisions**: (1) **`can_sink` beside `can_source`** — the one field §11 does not list. With §11's fields a pure input and an open-drain output are the same declaration (both rest released, neither sources; IBIS tells them apart by model type, `Input` against `Open_drain`), and the build must tell them apart: an input is a sense and an open drain is not (the supervisor's `~OUT`, the rails' status outputs and the optos' collectors would each have escalated their clusters as analog readers). Deriving it from `input` instead would make every CMOS input declare a port no datasheet gives. So a `Signal` pin that neither sources nor sinks is a **sense** from its declaration (`PinDecl::senses_at_build`): a digital one through its thresholds, and — the task's smallest replacement for `Analog`, recorded — an **analog reader** when it declares none, which asks for the solved voltage and escalates its cluster exactly as `Analog` did; a pin that drives, declares thresholds and idles released reads its net once it subscribes (`reads_when_subscribed`, exactly the old `DigitalBidir` + `Released`); every other signal pin declares no read. (2) **Thresholds' two forms in one struct**, the form chosen by the declaration beside it: fractions of the span `V(supply) − V(reference)` when the pin names a supply (the span is `V(supply)` in the engine's frame when it names no reference — the frame every published voltage is in until the sense task makes them relative), volts against the pin's reference when it names none; the build refuses fractions outside 0..=1 (or `v_il > v_ih`) on a supplied pin and non-finite or inverted volts on the rest, so an absolute pair handed to a supplied pin is refused, not read as a multiple of the supply. `PinHandle::thresholds()` reports them in volts at the instant — a relative declaration scaled by the voltage its supply's net names (`Analog(v)`; floating, fought, a clock or a level without volts reports none); the handle carries the declaration behind one `Arc` word (inline it grew `RecordedCallback` past clippy's variant-size bound). (3) **A figure in neither form, or a relative one with no supply pin to scale by, is made absolute at the corner of the datasheet's supply range where it holds at every supply**: the APS6404L's `V_IH = VDD − 0.4 V` at `VDD` max 3.6 → 3.2 V, `V_IL` 0.4 V (Table 9); the W25Q128JV's 0.3/0.7 × `VCC` on its four-wire bench facade → 0.81/2.52 V (2.7–3.6 V, §9.2); the SD card's 0.25/0.625 × `VDD` → 0.675/2.25 V (Table 6-2, 2.7–3.6 V). (4) **A pin whose part names no threshold of its own passes the JESD8C.01 pair with the reason at the call site**: the P2's `RESN`, `TEST` and `XI` (the datasheet's one threshold row, p. 47, is a fraction of a bank's `Vxxyy`, and these pins belong to no bank — p. 6), the native core's bridged pins (they are P2 pads, but `McuComponent` declares no bank-supply pin to scale by; its pads drive at the crate's nominal high, the P2 package's documented gap), the host PTY's and the QEMU node's receivers (bench adapters), the end switch's `COM` and the stepper drive's `STEP`/`DIR`/`ENA` (mechanism models with no datasheet — they keep reading with `Config::input_threshold_volts` until a receiver projects its own sense, where the two must fold into one), and the bench fixtures. (5) **Rail enables** read through their configuration's `EnableSpec` pair, absolute against the ground pin, the lower figure as `V_IL` and the higher as `V_IH`, hysteresis 0 — none of the AP62301, NCP114, XL1509 and UCC12040 sheets names a hysteresis apart from the pair; the SN74LVC1G14 declares its `ΔV_T` min 0.56 V (§5.5, `LVC1G14_HYSTERESIS_VOLTS`, carried on the gate's `Config::hysteresis_volts`), the 74LVC2G04 none (Table 7). (6) **`input` and `clamps` are declared, not stamped**: stamping either moves resolution, which a declarations task may not, and no part on the three boards has a clamp curve its datasheet gives; the solver takes them with the operating-point rules (the rules task), `capacitance_pf` in phase 6. The AM26LV32 declares **no** input port: its `r_I` is named (12 kΩ typ, 7 kΩ min, SLLS202H §6.5) and is the open fail-safe's mechanism (§8.4.1), but the voltage it holds an open input at is not — Figure 6-1 crosses zero current across a flat band of common-mode voltage, and Figure 8-2's equivalent input names resistors with no reading — so a `v_bias` would be invented (rule 6); open, and the part's IBIS model (its input I–V table) is where the number lives. (7) **The domain lints read power pins' references only** (`system.rs` `is_power`): a signal pin now declares a reference for its thresholds and its sense, and a signal measured against a ground nothing holds is that ground's supply pins' finding, not one more per signal — so the lints mean what phase 4 proved them with. New power-pin references: the P2's `VDD` and sixteen `VIO_a_b` against `GND` (p. 7: "all VDD and all Vxxyy pins must have closely-located bypass caps to GND"), the ADS122U04's `AVDD`/`DVDD`, the gates', PSRAM's, flash's, card's, TCXO's and 6N137/VO2631 supplies. The findings of the four reference systems (the EC32MB from its fingers, the DS2 add-on, the Edge board under the bench rails and with the module) are identical to phase 4's, finding for finding (a probe over both trees: 217 = 217, no line differs). (8) On a power-in or passive pin `with_idle(None)` is now the absence of a declaration, so `build_fixed_point`'s two "released on a slotless pin is refused" cases (four test instances) are gone; a Thevenin idle there is still refused, both routes. (9) **The P2 pads keep reading at subscription.** Declaring them senses at build is the fix the periodic record names for the one-in-forty sixth escalated solve, but it moves `FloatingSense` for every pad no core subscribes and the ROM boot's escalation count, which this task holds; it belongs to the sense task, which replaces the subscription anyway. **Datasheets cited new or corrected**: ADS122U04 `V_IL`/`V_IH` 0.3/0.7 × `DVDD` (SBAS752B §6.5; the model's "§7.3" citations were §6.3/§6.5 and are corrected); W25Q128JV Rev. F §9.4 (0.3/0.7 × `VCC`); APS6404L Table 9; SD Part 1 Physical Layer Specification v2.00 §6.6.1 Table 6-2 (the Simplified Specification leaves §6.6 blank); ISO67xx SLLSFJ6G §7.3 ratios on each input's own side; 6N137 `V_EL`/`V_EH`; P2 pads 0.3/0.7 × their bank's `VIO_a_b` (p. 47); AM26LS31 SLLS114N §5.3 and AM26LV32 SLLS202H §6.3/§6.5 for the test tree's line driver and receiver, whose provenance blocks carried "from memory" literature numbers (SLLS103, SLLS329), now pinned. **Proofs**: goldens — all four — byte-identical; census 87/6/0/3, 176/29/0/33, 22/2/0/0 unchanged; ROM boot 16 901 edges, 16 953 yields/publishes, 5 escalated solves, START 2.5 ms, and its wall time within noise of the periodic tree's (interleaved, five runs each on this host under load 11–15: 0.498 s → 0.510 s mean; debug); `board/tests/pin_declarations.rs` (stepped, 8 cases): a receiver's relative thresholds read 0.99/2.31 V on a 3.3 V bank, 0.54/1.26 V once it browns out to 1.8 V, and nothing once it is released, while an absolute pair beside it reads 0.8/2.0 V throughout; a reference or a supply naming an undeclared pin is `PinFacadeMismatch` naming it, netlist and bench; the JESD pair on a supplied pin is `InvalidDeclaration`; a `PowerOut` naming its reference is its own one-node cluster holding exactly 3.3 V with its load `Pulled(High, 10 000)`; `component::tests` holds the read rules case by case (seven declarations) and the scaling of every figure. **What MaD needs at the pin bump** (read-only here; its literals predate phase 1's `idle` too, so each is rewritten, not patched): `SIL/p2iss/src/lib.rs` — drop `PinKind` from the import (:66); TX (:927) → `PinDecl::digital_out(pin_name(link.tx_pin))`, RX (:934) → `PinDecl::digital_in(pin_name(link.rx_pin), JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS)` with the native core's reason; `with_level_pins` (:982, `DigitalBidir` idling high) → `PinDecl::digital_out(pin_name(pin)).with_thresholds(JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS)` (still driven at attach, so it declares no read, as before), and its doc (:969) no longer names a kind; `with_input_pins` (:1012) → `digital_in(.., JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS)`; `with_pulse_pins` (:1040) → `digital_out`. `SIL/p2iss/src/host_pty.rs` (:22, :77, :84) → `digital_out("TX")`, `digital_in("RX", JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS)` (the crate's own `HostPty`'s choice). `SIL/p2iss/src/flashnode.rs` (:24, `decl` :35 and its four rows :29–:32) → `digital_in(n, embsim_models::spi_flash_component::W25Q128JV_INPUT_THRESHOLDS_ANY_VCC)` for `CLK`/`CS`/`MOSI` (the facade has no `VCC` pin), `digital_out("MISO")`; `SIL/p2iss/src/sdnode.rs` (:30, `decl` :42, rows :36–:39) → the same with `sd_card_component::SD_INPUT_THRESHOLDS_ANY_VDD`. `PinDecl::analog(..)` for the analog probes: `SIL/p2iss/tests/rom_boot_net.rs:69`, `rom_serial_net.rs:73`, `sd_node.rs:76`, `SIL/MaDSim/src/iss_description.rs:139` and `:524`, `system_description.rs:158` and `:165` (`LOAD_CELL_PINS`, a `const` array — the constructors are `const fn`), `main.rs:246`. `digital_out`/`digital_in(.., JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS)`: `SIL/p2iss/tests/protocol_on_levels.rs:135`, `:142`; `level_pins.rs:70`, `:190`, `:197`. Every one of those files drops `PinKind` from its `embsim_board` import (24 literals, 29 `PinKind::` variants, 43 mentions with the imports and the one doc line).
   *`Sense` and the receiver's projection done 2026-09-25* (delivery: what a node sees; the engine's records do not move). **A node never reads the engine's projection.** `on_sense(id, Fn(Sense))` hands every subscriber `Sense { volts: Option<Volts>, periodic: Option<PeriodicSense { hi, lo: Option<Volts>, segment }>, at_ns }` (`component.rs`); the level is the receiver's own — `Sense::level(&Thresholds, last)`, `Thresholds::project`, `PinHandle::level` (the pin's declared thresholds at the instant), `DigitalReceiver` (a model's per-pin last level, an atomic); `PinHandle::sense()` reads the same `Sense` on demand. `NetState`, `level_of` and the JESD8C.01 pair stay the engine's **report** — `BuiltSystem::net_state`, the event log, the goldens, the census, and two instruments a bench probe records: `ComponentNetIo::on_net_report` and `PinHandle::net_report` (no model in `models/`, `boards/`, `qemu/` or `p2-qemu/src` uses either — `grep`). **The engine** keeps a voltage beside every state it resolves, `Net::volts: NetVolts { dc, phases }` (crate-private): `RootOutcome::volts` (a projected root's winning source's open-circuit voltage, a solved root's operating point, a terminal fight's), `PhaseRoot::volts`, `combine_phases` returning each root's `NetVolts` (the high phase's for rules 3/5, both phases' for rule 6, none for rules 1/2/4), `Arrival::swing` (a coupled root carries the source's two port voltages) — published beside the states in a lock-free `VoltsTable` (three atomic words a net, the engine thread its one writer). A delivery hands the subscription a `Delivery { state, node, reference }` from the pass itself (`EngineCore::deliver_sense`), and `PinHandle::measure` — the one conversion — turns it into the pin's `Sense`; the build's fixed point hands the same `Delivery` from its nets, and a registration on the inert link reads the build's tables (`PinHandle::published`), so the two code paths share one function. **The change gate** (`NetMove`): a sense is delivered when its net's state changes (logged `NetResolved` + `SenseDelivered`, as before), when **only the voltage behind it** changes (`SenseDelivered` alone — a `Driven(High)` whose source moved from 3.3 V to 1.8 V), and when the pin's **reference** moves while its own net did not (`RegisterSense` carries the reference; `reference_subs`, dense by net like `sense_subs` now is); a supply move re-delivered nothing (the review adds it: a relative threshold's supply net re-delivers the pin, `RegisterSense::supply`). None of the golden systems has a volts-only move or a referenced pin, so all four goldens are byte-identical, and the ROM boot delivers 51 432 senses on 35 059 state moves, 0 volts-only, 0 by reference (counted, then removed). **Decisions**: (1) **The shape.** `periodic` beside `volts` is the smallest shape that carries the segment (§11 contract line 6), with each phase's voltage so a receiver projects the phases through its own thresholds (`PeriodicSense::levels`, the isolator's relay); a periodic node's `volts` is `None`. (2) **The frame**: volts against the pin's declared `reference`, the engine's frame when it declares none; a reference that names no voltage — floating, only an unmodelled rail, a clock, or a pin a scenario detached (`SenseFrame::Detached`) — hands the pin nothing: **no implicit ground** (rule 6). The benches that held no ground now hold one: `p2-qemu`'s `crystal_pll`/`pad_modes` (`BENCH.GND`), `p2_package` (`grounded`), `w25q128jv` (`VCC` 3.3 V and `VSS`), `psram_spi` (the `J203.43` finger), `ads122u04_stepped` (`DGND`, `AVSS`). A part that **drives** at a voltage it senses (the ISO67xx, the gates, the ADS122U04's TX, the P2 pads at their bank) drives it above 0 V in the engine's frame — exact while its ground sits at 0 V there, as every board's does; a ground offset is not modelled (the ISO67xx docs say so). (3) **A fought node** with one operating point is handed that voltage — the one the `AmbiguousLevel` beside the `Contention` names, or the winner's where a strong source lost by the ratio — and the receiver projects it (JESD's band: no level; a 1.8 V bank's pad at 1.65 V: high). A node with **two** operating points (a clock fought, two rates meeting — `combine_phases` rules 1/2, the overlay's contention) is handed `None`, which a part cannot tell from open; recorded, and the findings are where fought and floating stay apart. (4) **An unmodelled rail** (a `PowerOut` at NaN) names no voltage: its node is handed `None` and a supply gate reads it down (rule 6); the engine's report keeps `Pulled(High, path)`. (5) **The dead band**: `DeadBand::{HoldLast, Unknown}` is a field of `Thresholds`, required by `Thresholds::new` — no crate default; `JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS` becomes `jesd8c01_lvcmos_thresholds(DeadBand)`, the caller naming the policy. **HoldLast** where the datasheet names a hysteresis: the SN74LVC1G14 (§5.5 `ΔV_T`), and every rail enable (`rail.rs` `enable_thresholds`: a comparator between its rising and falling figures keeps its state). **Unknown** for a plain CMOS input, where the datasheet guarantees neither level: the 74LVC2G04 (Table 7 — its in-band output is now **released**, the gate's answer for no level, where the model held; `logic_gate_levels` splits into the Schmitt case and `a_plain_input_reads_no_level_inside_its_band`), the SD card, W25Q128JV, APS6404L, ADS122U04, ISO67xx (the function table's indeterminate input: the default output), 6N137 enable, P2 pads (the core keeps its input bit), the test tree's AM26LS31/AM26LV32, and every JESD caller in the tree. (6) **The projection rule** (`Thresholds::project`): at or below `V_IL` low and at or above `V_IH` high whatever was read last; between, a last high stays high down to `V_IH − ΔV_T` (`V_T−` sits at least `ΔV_T` below `V_T+`, which is at most `V_IH`) and a last low stays low up to `V_IL + ΔV_T`; the rest is the policy's; a `DigitalReceiver` remembers the projection's result, `None` included. (7) **Supply gates read volts** against their cited windows: `isolation::{supply_volts, supply_up}(&Sense, min)`; `rail_volts`, `threshold_level`, the ISO67xx's and the gates' `nominal_supply_volts` (and `DEFAULT_NOMINAL_SUPPLY_VOLTS`), the ADS's `NOMINAL_DVDD_VOLTS`, the opto's `enable_high_min_volts` (it reads its enable pin's declared thresholds), the end switch's `input_threshold_volts`/`high_volts` (a closed contact reproduces the voltage `COM` is handed), the stepper's `input_threshold_volts` and `machine::{digital_level, DEFAULT_INPUT_THRESHOLD_VOLTS}` are deleted — the fold the PinRole record's decision (4) named. The ADS122U04 re-projects a held `~RESET` on a `DVDD` move; the supervisor's comparator reads `V_CC − V_SS` as handed; the rail's input and enable are handed volts against its ground, and with no ground the enable is undecided, a driven one included (the rail test says so). The P2 package reads `VDD` as volts only (`P2ResetState::vdd`, the engine's level, is deleted), `RESN` through its thresholds, the banks from `VIO_a_b` as handed, and hands its core `Option<Level>` from each pad's receiver (`P2Pads::on_pad_sense`); the QEMU core's logic is unchanged. (8) **The ADS122U04's UART drives its high at `DVDD`** (`V_OH` 0.8 × `DVDD` min, SBAS752B §6.5; `SerialLevelBridge::set_high_volts`): with the add-on's `DVDD` at 5 V the crate's 3.3 V TX sat inside the ISO6731's 0.3/0.7 × 5 V band and no reply crossed — a model gap the receiver's projection exposed. The test tree's ISO6731 drives each side's outputs at that side's sensed supply, its supplies read at the family's 1.71 V; the AM26LS31 powers at `V_POR` max 3.04 V (SLLS114N §5.5), the AM26LV32 at `V_CC` min 3 V (SLLS202H §6.3). (9) **`IC14`'s secondary ground is unconnected on the EdgeBoard** (`edgeboard.rs` asserts the defect): measured against its own `GND2`, the part's secondary side is down and `OUTA`/`OUTB`/`OUTC` float — the bench behaviour the defect predicts. `isolation_bridge` and `board_elements`' enable-transistor bench wire `IC14.9` to `EN_GND` (`J21.8`), the rework, said out loud in each rig; both Edge reference systems now report `FloatingSense` on `Net-(IC14-OUTA)` and `Net-(IC14-OUTB)` (the `AM26LS31`'s inputs) — the four findings by which the reference systems' 217 become 221, every other line identical (the PinRole probe, re-run). The force-path machine test also waits for the P2's bank rail `VIO_00_07` before its first command: a pad in an unpowered bank reads nothing, and the reply raced the module's 2.5 ms soft-start. (10) **Read declarations resolve only their own clusters** (`Resolver::declare_reads`): a `DeclareRead` batch was a full pass that re-solved every element cluster once per batch, and the batches were the attaching thread's timing — the one-in-forty sixth solve both earlier records left open, which this task's timing made one-in-one. Now the declared net's cluster is marked dirty and its sense list extended in the cached topology (a stale topology still takes the full pass), so the ROM boot escalates **3** solves, deterministically — the FET once at the start pass and the two bucks' dividers — where it asserted 5; `ESCALATED_SOLVES` is 3, `DESIGN.md` rule 4 and `TESTING.md` say so. The P2 pads still declare their read at subscription (PinRole decision (9)); declaring it at build is no longer needed for determinism. (11) **`FloatingSense` stays the engine's finding on the net's state**: a digital sense on a `Floating` net is reported, as an analog reader on one is (`kind: Analog`), on the build path and the live path alike; a sense handed `None` for any other reason in (2)–(4) — an unmodelled rail, two operating points, a reference with no voltage — raises nothing new, since the engine's report of the net is not `Floating`. **Proofs**: `board/tests/receiver_projection.rs` (stepped, 3 cases): one node walked 0 → 1.2 → 3.3 → 1.2 → 0 → 1.2 → 1.5 V under four receivers — the holding SN74LVC1G14 reads the 1.2 V node low, high, low; the Schmitt without memory reads the same second 1.2 V as no level where the holding one reads high; at 1.5 V a P2 pad in a 1.8 V bank reads high and the LVCMOS receiver no level; an analog reader whose reference pin is held at 1.0 V is handed 2.3 V and −0.5 V for 3.3 V and 0.5 V; a digital sense on a net nothing drives is handed no voltage with its `FloatingSense`; a receiver on two 25 Ω pads' fight is handed 1.65 V and reads no level, `Contention` naming both and `AmbiguousLevel` 1.65 V beside it. `component::tests` (the projection rule, 8 cases; the measurement against a reference, 4 cases, the phases too); `logic_gate_levels` (the plain input released in its band, `t_pd` after); every model's unit tests ported to `Sense` values. Goldens — all four — byte-identical; census 87/6/0/3, 176/29/0/33, 22/2/0/0 unchanged; ROM boot 16 901 edges, 16 953 yields/publishes, START 2.5 ms, 3 escalated solves (5 → 3, decision (10)), and 60 000 states identical to p2core by the README recipe, re-run on the final tree. **Cost** (interleaved against the PinRole tree, same host): release 2.154 → 2.176 µs/edge (+1.0 %, eight runs each), debug 0.255 → 0.268 s (+5 %, six runs each) — the receiver's projection on every one of the boot's 51 432 deliveries, a relative one reading its bank supply from the table; a mutex per delivery, a mutex per pass and the subscriptions' hash lookups measured first and removed (the lock-free table, the delivery handed from the engine, dense subscription tables). **What MaD needs at the pin bump** (read-only here), beyond the periodic and PinRole lists: every `JESD8C01_LVCMOS_THRESHOLDS` those lists name becomes `jesd8c01_lvcmos_thresholds(DeadBand::Unknown)` (`p2iss/src/lib.rs` :934, :982, :1012; `host_pty.rs` :84; `tests/protocol_on_levels.rs` :142; `level_pins.rs` :190, :197), importing `DeadBand`. `on_sense` hands `Sense`: `p2iss/src/lib.rs` — the level pins (:1152, `level_of(state)` :1159) take a `DigitalReceiver::new(io.pin(pin_name(pin))?)` and `receiver.read(&sense)`; the UART RX (:1179, `level_of` :1185) the same, with `now = sense.at_ns`; drop `level_of` from the import (:65). `p2iss/src/flashnode.rs` (import :23; `CS` :121/:122, `MOSI` :132/:133, `CLK` :141/:142) and `sdnode.rs` (import :29; `CS` :144/:145, `MOSI` :166/:167, `CLK` :180/:181): one `DigitalReceiver` per pin, `level_of(state)` → `rx.read(&sense)`. `SerialLevelBridge::receive_sense` takes the RX pin and the sense: `p2iss/src/host_pty.rs` :131/:135 and `tests/protocol_on_levels.rs` :176/:177 → `let rx = io.pin("RX")?; io.on_sense("RX", move |sense| … bridge.receive_sense(&rx, &sense))`. MaD's pins declare no reference, so none of its benches needs a ground; its `on_sense` callbacks are its only `NetState` readers (no `PinHandle::sense`, `Thresholds::new`, `P2ResetState` or supply helper in `p2iss`/`MaDSim`). **Open, with an owner**: the operating-point precedence (decision (a)) and the single-source rule are the rules task's, with the two analog goldens; `InputPort`/`Clamp` stamping likewise; the AM26LV32's input bias (its IBIS model); a ground offset under a part that drives at a sensed supply.
   *The resolution rules done 2026-09-25* (§10 "Resolution", §11 contract line 4; retires phase 1's decision (a)). **A reader no longer silences a fight.** A cluster solved because an analog reader, a current injection or a current instrument asked publishes each root's operating point **and** reports rule 2's fights beside it exactly as the cluster would without the reader: two strong drivers disagreeing across less than the pull bar (`Contention`, with `AmbiguousLevel` inside the band), a strong source that lost by the ratio while disagreeing, and two terminal sources fighting on one root (the terminal's `own_fight`: the finding, and `AmbiguousLevel` inside the band). The `precedence` flag is gone from both resolvers (`resolve_cluster`, and the test-only `resolve_reference`, so the incremental-vs-full oracle holds the same rule); `PhaseRoot::of`'s `fought` also reads `ambiguous`, so a periodic cluster under a reader combines its phases (`combine_phases` rule 2) exactly as it does without one. **Decisions**: (1) **One rule for every solve on request.** The instrument path already published the operating point with the fights beside it; the analog reader and the injection now take that path, and so does a terminal's own fight under any of them — under an instrument or an element it published `Contention` inside the band, and it now publishes the operating point with the finding and `AmbiguousLevel` beside it (no test or golden held the old form). The state stays `Analog(v)`: the reader is handed the voltage by its `Sense` (the sense task's decision (3)), and the finding carries the fault. **The two goldens, re-blessed** (rule 7; `EMBSIM_BLESS=1`, the only re-bless of this phase): `net_stuck_shared_node` 17 → 19 lines — `finding Contention { net: "DRV.A", drivers: [DRV.A] }` after `drive_applied seq=0` (A's 3.3 V at 23.7 Ω loses to the stuck 0 V terminal by the ratio while disagreeing, the fight rule 2 reports whenever no reader reads the node), and `finding Contention { net: "DRV.A", drivers: [DRV.A, DRV.B] }` after `seq=1` (B's 0.19 V at 31.1 Ω joins the strong slots the finding names; the bus dedups on equality, so the grown set is a second finding and nothing after it is new); no `AmbiguousLevel`, 0 V being outside the band; every other line identical, the probe's `analog:0uv` included — the terminal holds the node whoever fights it. `nominal_analog_cluster` 63 → 75 lines — `finding Contention { net: "DRV.A", drivers: [DRV.A, DRV.B] }` and `finding AmbiguousLevel { net: "DRV.A", volts: 1.9549817518248174 }` after `seq=1`, then one `AmbiguousLevel` after each of `seq=2` … `seq=11` carrying that pass's solved voltage (1.862043 … 1.771188 V, all inside 0.8–2.0 V; the `Contention` is the same finding every pass and is reported once); every `net_resolved` and `sense` line is the same text, shifted by the lines before it. The volts are the same because the solve, its inputs and its card order are unchanged — only the findings the precedence dropped are kept — and `seq=0`'s `analog:3300000uv`, now the single-source rule's (one source, unsolved), is the same number exactly. `serial_levels` and `wake_ladder` byte-identical. **The single-source rule** (`DESIGN.md` rule 8): an analog reader's root that exactly one numeric source reaches — a slot or a terminal, by rule 2's reach — is handed that source's open-circuit voltage, `Analog(v_oc)` with `volts` = `v_oc`, and nothing is solved: no other slot, terminal or injection shares its conduction component (a second would reach it), so no current flows there and the voltage is exact. A root two or more sources reach asks the solver, lazily, once per phase; an injection, an instrument or an element still solves the whole cluster up front (`eager`) — a current or a region needs the solve. Both resolvers. (2) **The state is `Analog(v_oc)`**, not the ranking's `Driven`/`Pulled`: an analog-read root reports a voltage whether it solved or not, so no report moves, only the solve count. Measured: the ROM boot escalates **3**, unchanged, and each is real — none is a reader's: the element cluster at `VIN_Edge_Protected` (two elements between two terminals; an element cluster always solves) and the bucks' feedback dividers `Net-(U402-FB)` and `Net-(U403-FB)` (no slot source; the output rail and ground reach each as disagreeing pulls, which rule 5 solves). The four reference systems' build solves are 0, 1, 63 and 65 with the rule on and with it switched off (the probe re-run both ways): where the rule acts is an analog reader one source reaches — the nominal golden's first pass, an AM26LV32 input no encoder drives, a bench ADC on a rail's resistor. **The declarations, stamped** (§10; `capacitance_pf` stays declared-only until phase 6). An **input port** is a permanent Thevenin source at the pin, `v_bias` behind `r_in` (`Resolver::add_port`: a `DriveSlot` with `port: true`, no endpoint a component holds, never republished, left out of `driving_pins`), ranked by rule 2 like any source — at 1 kΩ or more a pull, so an otherwise open input sits at its bias and any driver wins. The build refuses one on a pin that is neither `Signal` nor `PowerIn`, or without a finite bias and a finite positive `r_in` (`InvalidDeclaration`). (3) **`v_bias` is stamped in the engine's frame**, exact while the part's reference sits at 0 V — the assumption the sense task's decision (2) makes for a part that drives at a voltage it senses; a port behind a floating ground is not modelled. (4) **A port's current is the pin's own load, not summed into `sense_current`/`pin_current`**: no caller reads one, and a second endpoint on `PinHandle` would widen every handle for it. A **clamp** is a `PwlCurve::Diode { vf, r_d }` branch — pin to supply for `ClampRail::Supply`, reference to pin for `ClampRail::Reference` (`component::clamp_branches`) — registered through the same `register_branch` as the part's own branches, on both routes, so the region loop decides it and its current is summed into the pin's. A clamp ending on a declared terminal is an element with a boundary end: no union, no census change; a clamp to a pin on a net that is not a terminal would join that net's cluster as any element does — a census change, and none arises: no part on the three boards declares a clamp. A clamp makes its cluster an element cluster, solved whenever it resolves — the declaring part's cost alone. The build refuses a negative or non-finite knee or resistance. **The AM26LV32's port**: each input's open-circuit voltage is SLLS202H Figure 9-2, "RS422 Port Open-Circuit Voltage vs V_CC" (§9.2.3 Application Curve) — A 0.83 V, B 0.70 V, flat from `V_CC` 1.6 V to 3.6 V (read against the figure's 0.2 V grid: 0.829 V and 0.697 V) — behind `r_I` 12 kΩ typical (§6.5): `AM26LV32_A_PORT`/`_B_PORT` in the test tree's `machine_parts`, on pins 1, 2, 6, 7, 9 and 10. This is the bias the PinRole record's decision (6) and the sense record's "open" found missing: they read Figure 6-1 and Figure 8-2, and Figure 9-2 names it. An open pair reads +130 mV, inside ±200 mV — high, by the fail-safe, through the part's own bias and no other source; below `V_CC` 1.6 V the figure's curves fall (0.42/0.35 V at 0.8 V), which a port stamped once does not follow. The encoder case of `machine_system` now reads `A+` at the encoder's level divided against the port — (V·12 000 + 0.83·25) / 12 025, 1.7 mV and 3.2932 V — and says so. The reference systems lose the twelve `FloatingSense { kind: Analog }` on `A±`, `B±` and `ZI±` (six per Edge system): the receiver's own ports bias its open inputs — 221 findings become 209, every other line identical. (5) **No regulator declares a port.** The quiescent load §10 names is a current on every regulator sheet on the boards, and `r_in = V / I_Q` holds at one voltage the sheet does not tie to a bias: the mechanism is there (a `PowerIn` port is legal) and nothing is invented for it (rule 6). **`Finding::OpenDrainWithoutPullUp`**, at build (`system.rs` `open_drains_without_pull_up`, after the other build lints): a `Signal` pin that sinks and cannot source, whose net's reach (`Resolver::reached_roots`: its cluster's roots and boundary terminals at a finite series resistance, a terminal ending every path — rule 2's reach) finds no pull-up — a `PowerOut` pin that is not its part's declared reference (a rail, not a ground), a harness supply or `net_stuck` above 0 V or unmodelled, a signal pin that sources, an input port biased above 0 V (the review drops the port: a load, not a pull-up). It asks the declarations and the network, never the settled states: a rail still in its soft-start at the snapshot is the pull-up the board wires. (6) **§10's "through less than `WEAK_DRIVE_OHMS`" is read as rule 2's "reaches"**: a pull-up is by rule 2's own definition a source at 1 kΩ or more, so the literal bound fires on every pulled-up open drain — on `P2_RESN` among them, whose pull-up is `R100`, 10.5 kΩ to `VIO_56_63`. (7) **A ground is not a pull-up** (an open drain with only a pull-down raises the finding), and **an open drain whose reach finds no other part's pin raises nothing** — a no-connect stub, or a net that leaves the board only through a connector, whose pull-up is the far side's: nothing on the board reads it. On the reference systems the lint asks nine open drains and raises nothing: the STM1061's `~OUT` (`U404`) reaches `VIO_56_63` through `R100`; the eight VO2631 collectors (`U5`–`U8`) reach `VIO_16_23` through their pull-ups (the harness finger at the module LDO's 3.3 V alone, the module's `U503` in the assembly); skipped are `U4`'s (6N137) collector, which leaves the board on `J10` (the charge-pump connector), and `IC3`/`IC4`'s (UCC12040) `SYNC_OK`, unconnected. The plan's "I2C nets on the module" carry no pin any model declares open-drain (the P2's pads source), so there the lint has nothing to ask. **Proofs**: `board/tests/resolution_rules.rs` (stepped, 9 cases) — two 25 Ω pads fighting under an ADC input: the input is handed 1.65 V, the net reads `Analog(1.65)`, `Contention` names both pads and `AmbiguousLevel` 1.65 V sits beside it; a 3.3 V rail against a short on its net, read directly: `Analog(1.65)` with one `Contention` naming no pin and its `AmbiguousLevel` (build); a rail through one 10 kΩ resistor into an ADC input: handed exactly 3.3 V with **0** solves, then — a 25 Ω pad driving the node low — 3.3 × 25 / 10 025 V from exactly **1** solve; an AM26LV32 powered at 3.3 V and enabled with channel 1 open: `1A` `Analog(0.83)`, `1B` `Analog(0.70)`, 0 solves, `1Y` `Driven(High)`, then a 25 Ω pad on `1A` pulls it to 0.83 × 25 / 12 025 V (1.7 mV) and `1Y` goes `Driven(Low)`; a pin clamped to its 3.3 V supply (knee 0.7 V, 10 Ω — a bench clamp, no part's) driven through 1 kΩ: 3 V and no current at 3 V, then held at 4.0099 V with the 0.99 mA the kilohm passes at 5 V, the supply's net a cluster of its own; the open-drain fixture, four cases: read with no resistor → the finding naming `B.U1`, pin 1, `B.OUT`; pulled down only → the same; pulled up to a rail → none; a no-connect → none. `cluster_census::no_reference_system_has_an_open_drain_without_a_pull_up` (the four systems); `pin_declarations::a_port_or_clamp_the_solver_cannot_stamp_is_refused` (4 declarations × both routes); `engine::tests` — `an_analog_sense_reads_the_operating_point_of_a_fought_node` now asserts the two findings, `analog_sense_escalates_sourced_cluster_but_pull_up_stays_pulled` exactly 3.3 V and 0 solves. Goldens: the two above re-blessed, `serial_levels`/`wake_ladder` byte-identical; census 87/6/0/3, 176/29/0/33, 22/2/0/0 unchanged (a port adds a slot, not an edge; no clamp is declared); ROM boot 16 901 edges, 16 953 yields/publishes, START 2.5 ms, **3** escalated solves, and 60 000 states identical to p2core by the README recipe (the p2core reference reused from the sense task's run — p2core did not move). **Cost**: ROM boot wall time interleaved against the sense tree (six runs each, debug, this host): 0.2670 → 0.2690 s mean, +0.7 %, within noise. **What MaD needs at the pin bump**: nothing beyond the three lists above. `Finding` gains a variant, and MaD matches none exhaustively (`MaDSim/src/system_description.rs:239` logs them); MaD declares no port, clamp or sink-only pin; its analog readers (`LOAD_CELL_PINS`, the `p2iss` probes) are handed the same voltages, now with any fight on their nets reported beside them and a single source's voltage unsolved. **Open**: the ports' fall below `V_CC` 1.6 V and `r_I`'s 7 kΩ minimum; a port or a clamp behind a ground that is not at 0 V; a pin current that includes its port.
   *The P2 items done 2026-09-25* (what phases 2–4 handed to phase 5 — the phase-4 record's decisions (15), (18) and (21), its open `Finding::BrownoutWithoutReset` and current-source modes — and `sil-unified-drive.md` step 5). **Every core is held the same way, and the chip restarts when the datasheet says.** `P2_RESTART_DELAY_NS` = 3 000 000 (`boards/src/p2.rs`; P2X8C4M64P Datasheet 2022/11/01, "Pin Descriptions", p. 6, the `RESN` row: "When low, resets the Propeller: all cogs disabled and I/O pins floating. Propeller restarts 3 ms after RESn transitions from low to high"). The reset **releases** the first instant the gate's condition holds — the system live, `RESN` reading released, `VDD` inside 1.7–1.9 V, every bank told (`P2Package::follow_reset`, on every `RESN`, `VDD` and bank delivery and at `Component::start`) — and the package arms a wake of **its own** 3 ms on; a re-assertion before then forgets the release (a stale deadline wake finds a later release, or none, and does nothing); at the deadline `try_begin` starts the core if the reset has stayed released, lands its held wakes at that instant and arms its held periods. `StartState` gains `Restarting { released_at_ns, starts_at_ns }`. `P2Core::start` now always runs on the engine thread, inside the package's wake; with no virtual clock to count on the package says so and the core stays held. **One entry point for a core's time**: `ComponentNetIo::with_wake_gate(Arc<dyn WakeGate>)` (`board/src/component.rs`; `WakeGate::{on_wake_ns, schedule_at_ns, schedule_every_ns}`, `WakeHandler`) routes a hosted node's wake handler and schedules — the µs forms too — to its host and changes nothing else; the package's `CoreWakes` is the one host: a handler stored, a request before START held, one after forwarded, one after a brownout dropped. `P2Pads::{on_wake_ns, schedule_at_ns}` are the gated net I/O's, and the native core's `Component::attach(self, pads.io)` is handed the same gated I/O — so its handler no longer replaces the package's (the obstacle phase 4's decision (15) named), and the engine holds exactly one handler for the package: the package's, which counts the delay and forwards the core's. **The native core's pads through `BankSupplies`** (`board/src/mcu.rs`): `McuComponent::host_pads(PadPorts)`, `PadPorts = Arc<dyn Fn(&'static str, Level) -> Option<TheveninDrive>>` (`None` releases the pad); hosted, every bridged pad drives through the host's port — the GPIO outputs, the step channels' two phases (`step_drive`; a phase with no supply releases the pin), the serial TX at every bit (`SerialLevelBridge::with_ports`, read per bit) — and nothing is published before `Component::start`, where the power-on state goes out (a GPIO output's level, a line's idle, a step channel's segment) before the entry spawns; an MCU on a board of its own is exactly as before (publishes from attach, `digital_drive`'s ports). The package's `P2Core` impl answers `BankSupplies::pad_drive(pad_of(pin), NATIVE_PAD_MODE, true, level == High)`. **`P2_FAST_OHMS` from the datasheet**: `P2_FAST_SOURCE_POINTS` (`Voh` relative to `Vxxyy`: −6 / −170 / −580 mV sourcing 1 / 10 / 30 mA) and `P2_FAST_SINK_POINTS` (`Vol`: 15 / 160 / 510 mV sinking the same) — "DC Characteristics", **p. 48**, the "Typ" column at a 3.3 V supply (the phase-4 record's p. 47 is the table's first page; these rows sit on its continuation) — fitted by `fitted_ohms` (`Σ V·I / Σ I²`, a `const fn`): 0.036021 W ÷ 0.002002 A² = **17.9925 Ω**, replacing the 25 Ω placeholder; sourcing alone fits 19.09 Ω, sinking 16.90 Ω, and the 10 / 30 mA rows' ratios span 16.0–19.33 Ω. **The brownout**: `watch_brownout` on every `RESN`/`VDD` delivery after START — `VDD` out of its window while `RESN` does not read low — records `StartState::BrownoutWithoutReset { started_at_ns, at_ns, reset }`, warns once, and calls `P2Core::reset` (new, default no-op): the QEMU core sets `Shared::held` and its wake returns before any slice — the stall path, for good (`P2QemuHandle::held`); the native core warns that its firmware thread runs on; the package's forwarder delivers the core nothing after it and `CoreWakes` drops its later requests. **Step 5**: `PulseSegment` is **`PeriodicSchedule { emitted, freq_hz, total, since_ns }`** (`peripherals/src/pulse_out.rs`, re-exported by `embsim_board`), documented as the half of a `Drive::Periodic` a peripheral owns; `emitted_at(now_ns)`, `completes_at()` and `rebased_at(at_ns)` in nanoseconds through one integration (named `emitted_at_ns`/`rebased_at_ns` since the review, so a microsecond caller fails to compile), `pulses_in(elapsed_ns, freq)` = ⌊elapsed·f / 10⁹⌋ in 128 bits (a 64-bit product saturates half an hour into a 10 MHz train); `PulseOut`'s channel state, `run()`, `emitted()`, `set_frequency` and `stop` count in the same nanoseconds, so the firmware's count and every subscriber's agree at any instant (`POLL_TICK_US` stays: a sleep length, not a count); the event log writes `since=…ns`; the oscillator anchors at its wake's nanosecond. **Decisions**: (1) **A core's time through its host's `WakeGate`** — the smallest form of "one entry point": a composition on the handle table a host hands its hosted node, not a message between node and engine (rule 2 unchanged: the gate forwards to the same wake scheduling), so a native core needed no second `attach`. (2) **What releases the reset** is the gate's whole condition, so a `VDD` entering its window under a `RESN` already released counts as the transition the datasheet names; a bench with its supplies up from the build starts at 3 ms (`crystal_pll`, `pad_modes`, `p2_package`). The datasheet's "within 5 ms, the Propeller 2 loads its Bootloader" (Operation, p. 10) is a bound, not an instant, and is not modelled. (3) **A release shorter than the delay starts nothing**, and the delay counts again from the next release. (4) **The native core's wakes are held until START** like any core's — in facade mode too, so a byte a test writes before START goes out after it (the machine force path waits for the bank rail and its bytes cross once the P2 has started; the test passes unchanged). (5) **Inside a package the native core publishes from START**: a chip in reset floats every pin (the `RESN` row), where a standalone MCU presents its power-on state from attach. The build snapshot never starts a core, so the bridged TX now floats there: `ec32mb_module::every_driver_is_a_registered_model` asserts no driven net on the module (the claim `one-driven-net` → `no-net-driven`), `machine_system::every_declared_finger_is_one_node_across_the_socket` asserts `EC32MB.P2_IO2`/`EdgeBoard.P2` one node, floating, and the assembled machine's findings gain `FloatingSense { net: "EdgeBoard.P2", kind: Digital }` — `IC5`'s input before the P2 runs, the one line by which 209 become 210 (the rules task's probe, re-run; every other line and every build solve count identical). (6) **The native core's pads drive in the fast mode both ways**, `NATIVE_PAD_MODE` = `P_HIGH_FAST | P_LOW_FAST` = 0: the word a pad holds until a `WRPIN` changes it (Silicon Documentation v35, "Pin Configuration Modes"), and the HAL tables the native core bridges name no mode. (7) **One fitted `P2_FAST_OHMS` for both levels**: the pad-mode table names one "fast" mode, a pad is a Thevenin port whose open-circuit voltages are the rails, so its impedance is the one figure left, and least squares through the origin over all six points weights the larger currents where the figure decides anything — no projection on the three boards moves (every fast pad meets 10 kΩ and more; ROM boot counts identical; `pad_modes`' 15 kΩ pull against a 25 Ω bench sink never involves it). The six points are the table's "Typ" column at a 3.3 V supply, and the one figure serves a 1.8 V bank as well: the datasheet gives no 1.8 V row (the review). (8) **The brownout is the package's report, not an engine `Finding`**: an engine finding needs a model-side report path, which phase 4's decisions (10) and (16) refused and rule 2's one interface does not carry, so `Finding::BrownoutWithoutReset` (§5's list) is not added; the report is handle-readable with a `tracing::warn`, as the START report is. (9) **`RESN` not asserted** is `RESN` not reading low: a floating `RESN` does not excuse a brownout. (10) **A held core stays held**: pads keep what they published (never a pad change, the §2 row), the package never starts it again, and a `RESN` pulse after START stays information for the core — a restart needs a core entry that re-runs the boot (QEMU's machine state, a native image's statics), which no core has; "Rebooting" (p. 35) is what it would implement. (11) **The current-source pad modes stay unmapped**: the datasheet names 1 mA / 100 µA / 10 µA ("Features", p. 2; the smart-pin mode table, p. 24) and no compliance, `Drive::Current` is an ideal injection (1 mA into a 10 kΩ pull-down would put 10 V on a 3.3 V pad), and nothing calls for one; `PadDrive::CurrentSource` stays the logged unmapped path and §10's `Current` row says so. (12) **The stepper plant runs on nanoseconds** (`now_ns`, `last_edge_ns`, `interval_ns`, `train_delivery_lag_ns`; its `Config` durations stay microseconds behind `NS_PER_US`): a microsecond plant folds the outgoing train at a floored instant, so a rate or `DIR` change between two microseconds would cost a 20 MHz train ten pulses — `a_rate_change_between_microseconds_folds_each_side_exactly` holds 21 010. (13) **A bank rail moving after START is not re-published** onto the pads a core drives (the QEMU core's rule, now the native core's): a pad reads the table when it next publishes; no board moves a bank after START. (14) Code that said "phase 5" meaning this phase now cites `NODES.md` §12 item 5; `net.rs`'s coupling-margin note, which meant §8's phase 5 (capacitors), names §12 item 6. **Proofs**: `p2_package.rs` (stepped, own binary) 17 → 25 cases — `the_core_starts_three_milliseconds_after_resn_rises` (`RESN` driven low from the build and released at 2 ms: `Restarting { 2 ms, 5 ms }` read inside a bench wake at 3 ms, the core started and first woken at exactly 5 ms), `a_reset_release_shorter_than_the_restart_delay_starts_nothing` (released 1–2 ms: `Held` with `RESN` low, unstarted and unwoken at 4.5 ms; released again at 6 ms: started at 9 ms), `a_core_rail_that_leaves_its_window_while_running_holds_the_core` (a core woken every millisecond from its 3 ms start, `VDD` 1.8 → 1.2 V at 6.5 ms: `BrownoutWithoutReset { 3 ms, 6.5 ms, RESN high, 1.2 V }`, `reset` at 6.5 ms, woken at 3–6 ms and not after; with `RESN` pulled low at 6.2 ms first: `Started`, no `reset`, woken on), `the_native_cores_pads_drive_at_their_banks_supply_from_the_start` (an owned-execution `McuComponent` whose entry sets a bridged GPIO: the pad floats at 1 ms, then sits at exactly 1.8 V / 3.3 V after START, or floats with its bank named when the bank is unsupplied), `the_fast_pad_strength_is_fitted_to_the_datasheet_output_table`; the existing START cases moved by the delay (`Started { at_ns: 3 ms }` from the build, `rail + 3 ms`); `boards` unit case `the_fast_impedance_is_the_fit_of_the_datasheet_figures`; `mcu::tests::a_step_drive_swings_between_the_pads_own_ports` (a host's ports, a phase with no supply); `pulse_out` — the integration in nanoseconds (a sub-microsecond anchor, one nanosecond short of a pulse, an hour of 10 MHz exact) and every rebasing case between microseconds; the stepper's sub-microsecond rate change. `crystal_pll`: started at 3 ms, the guest's PLL selection at 4 ms still stalls until the 5 ms clock, 4 edges 12–13 ns apart, 4 yields. ROM boot: `edges=16901 yields=16953 publishes=16953 escalated_solves=3 start_ns=Some(5500000)`, the reset releasing at 2.5 ms and START 3 ms on, no edge before it, no pad in an unpowered bank; **60 000 states identical to p2core** (`p2-qemu/README.md`'s recipe, the rules task's p2core reference reused — p2core did not move — `compared 60000 | dropped 0 | identical`). `pulse_bridge` 8/8 with every count exact and the reversal summing to the firmware's own count; `isolation_bridge` 6 552 / 655 360 pulses verbatim at 65 events (57 since the review). Goldens unmoved by this task (its diff of the traces empty); census unchanged. **Cost**: ROM boot 0.268 s debug against the rules task's 0.266–0.269 s. **What MaD needs** is folded into the phase's list above: `p2iss` as a `P2Core`, `P2Core::start` on the engine thread, the 3 ms restart, `PeriodicSchedule` in nanoseconds in `p2iss/src/pulse.rs`, and `sil-unified-drive.md`'s step 5 and its moved citations.
6. **Capacitors** (§8 phase 5). 7. **I2C bench** (§8 phase 6). 8. **Plant-driven edges** (§8 phase 7).

Each phase is one pull request, gated by its proof list and by the goldens passing without re-blessing; `DESIGN.md` §5 is the review checklist for every one of them.
